One application-wide binding, handled only for a pointer over a panel; the innermost panel that can scroll wins. It re-checks its content height 4 times a second (a re-wrapped label changes the wanted height without a size event). Used for the top part of the Selected procedure card (the run block and verification buttons are pinned below it) and for the Asset History, Standards Specs, Datasheet Builder pages (the Mudcats Datasheet Importer page was removed later) (`Manager._scroll_page`, content in `.content`). The Procedures table keeps its own scrollbar and is not inside a scroll panel; at 900x600 in the Adjustments view (note + wrapped bar) it shows about 3 rows.
- Verification text: the card shows the one-line status pill (verified.Status.pill, unchanged wording and colours) and a small info button; the full text (verified.Status.detail_lines, unchanged) opens in a small window under the button (`Manager.show_verified_details`: selectable, Esc closes it, closes when focus leaves). verified.py untouched.
- Row menu: right click (also middle button / Ctrl-click) on a Procedures row selects it, updates the card, and posts `ui.themed_menu` (built afresh each time, released with `ui.post_menu`); Shift+F10 / the Menu key posts it for the selected row. Entries (`procedure_menu.items`, Tk-free): Run procedure, Try in demo mode, Mark verified... / Unlock... (the card buttons' own text and state), Restore previous version..., Remove from library (its own confirmation / refusal for locked and user-built rows), Copy procedure name, Copy file path, Show file location (explorer /select, open -R, xdg-open of the folder; disabled without xdg-open). Run / demo follow the 'Ready to run' state. Not done: 'no Run while a run is active' (the Manager starts procedures as separate processes and does not track them), Export as package and the procmods editor (no entry point in the Manager today), and a Manual-view menu check on real Tk (the test data has no manual procedure). The Adjustments view uses the same wording as the other views ('Run procedure').
- Procedure Development tab REMOVED: library folder -> 'Library folder...' button on the Procedures bar (tooltip shows the folder or '(not set: the picker opens in the folder you used last)'; packages_ui text updated); Restore previous version... -> row menu (enabled for a managed script with a saved backup; same chooser, confirmation and backup-before-restore); Install update (.py)... and its helpers deleted from the UI (packages.install and the backups logic untouched); Test in demo mode duplicated Try in demo mode. `_backup`, `_atomic_replace`, `_available_backups`, `_validated_update` stay for the restore.
- 'Adjustment' view label is now 'Adjustments' (display only; stored kind 'adjustment').
- Fingerprints: no `Procedures/*.procedure.json` lists `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.py`, `ui_theme.py`, `procedure_menu.py` or `packages_ui.py` (grep), so no verified procedure changes state; the per-procedure status table printed before and after is identical.
- Tests: `tests/test_procedure_menu.py` (menu model, source checks; pinned block, short verify texts), `tests/test_verified_ui.py` (short text + `full_text`), `tests/test_manager_tabs.py` / `tests/test_packages_ui.py` (tab list, Library folder button), `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_manager_run_top` (buttons mapped and inside the window in the three views at 1267x860 and 900x600, card and page scrolling), `checks_manager_row_menu`, `checks_manager_verified_info`. Screenshots: set SHOT_DIR (`manager_run_in_card_<view>_<w>x<h>_<theme>.png`, `manager_<tab>_<w>x<h>_<theme>.png`, `manager_verified_info_*`).

## Standards spec editor (branch `spec-editor`; owner-approved draft; simulated bench / temp copies and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb, light and dark; never seen on Windows)
- Where: Standards Specs tab, button **Edit spec...** for the selected standard (the only change in existing code is that button and `SpecManagerPanel.edit_spec` in `specmanager_ui.py`; `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.py` is untouched).
- New modules (none is in any sidecar fingerprint list, so no procedure flips to 'Changed since verification'; `spec_editor.procedures_listing()` checks that live and `tests/test_spec_editor.py` asserts it for all 11 sheets):
  `spec_editor.py` (model: typed values with Decimal, uid-tracked diff, validation with an issue list per row), `spec_edit_io.py` (backup, atomic write, read-back, rebuild, restore, CSV/PDF export), `spec_edit_log.py` (log tables, hand-edit state), `spec_editor_ui.py` (Tk).
- Covers everything in the sheet file: rows (and add / delete row, add / delete period line), notes, standard info (confidence_pct -> divisor, revision, reference, approved_by ...), limits, sheet issues, and per-row details (variant, qualifiers, raw text, note refs). Typed text uses the SI prefixes and is parsed with Decimal; a wrong unit is refused, not guessed. Display scaling only.
- Validation: only errors INTRODUCED by the pending edit block saving (owner decision; `spec_editor.validate` compares the issue list with that of the file as loaded, per row / field, kind and text). An error the file already had stays red, is marked 'existing', and only warns in the Save dialog and the Pending panel; an edit that fixes an error, or leaves it alone, saves. Kinds of error (schema, numbers / units, spans, empty period, note ref that does not exist, confidence outside 0-100, empty note); warnings do not (feeds uncertainty budgets, printed text not updated after a numeric edit, a new row identical to an existing one, layout differs from the builders'). Rows / notes / info lines with an error are shown in RED (theme token `bad`) with an X icon and the issue text as tooltip and under the list; warnings are amber with a triangle. Live as validation changes.
- Save = 'Document & save edit...': reason, source and edited-by are required (no second approver, by the owner's decision). Order: verified backup `Backups/spec_editor/<name>_backup_vNN.json` (optional) -> temp file + `os.replace` ONCE -> read back and compare bytes and JSON -> log row (if that fails the file is put back) -> optional resync of the standard in `lab_registry.sqlite3`. A write is never retried; on a mismatch the file is restored from the backup/before-copy and reported.
- Log (additive, in `lab_registry.sqlite3` only): `spec_edits` (who, when, reason, source, backup file name, SHA-256 before / after, JSON diff, gzipped before-copy) and `spec_edit_events`; both are append-only by SQL triggers (UPDATE and DELETE are refused). `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` is never opened. 'Restore the file as it was before this edit...' is itself a documented edit (new row, reason required; a restore can be restored).
- Rebuild of the standards sheet in the database: an in-place resync of that standard's `spec_rows` (keeps the standard id, assets, divisor / period set in Standards Specs, `spec_changes` and the row ids of matched rows; edits made in Standards Specs are kept unless this edit changed the same field, then the edit wins and is listed in the change log). If the database does not match the file as it was (rows missing, standard not loaded) the rebuild is skipped and says so; the file is saved anyway.
- 'Edited by hand since built' marker (standard info and history): computed from the SHA chain: as built / edited by hand / back to as built / changed outside the editor.
- **Builders that would overwrite hand edits** (every builder writes its file unconditionally; re-running one loses the edits, the history keeps the before-copy so 'Restore' / 'Compare with current file' still work):
  coil `build_coil_standard.py`; current_amp_coil `build_current_amp_coil_standard.py`; light `build_light_standard.py`; 8563E `build_8563e_standard_from_manual.py`; 5520A `build_5520a_standard.py`; 5522A and 5560A `build_standard_spec.py`; 5790B `build_5790b_standard_from_manual.py`; 96270A `build_96270a_standard_from_manual.py`; 8902A `build_8902a_standard_from_manual.py`; 3458A `build_3458a_standard.py`. `specs/build_*.py` are not modified.
- History window: id, time, who, reason, source, backup file name, SHA-256 before / after, JSON diff; Compare with current file; Restore; Export history (PDF via reportlab and `report_pdf._fonts`, else CSV).
- Tests: `python -m unittest tests.test_spec_editor` (no Tk; includes pre-existing vs introduced errors: typed values, byte-identical round trip of all 11 sheets, diff / revert, per-row issue list, atomic write with failure injection, log failure, append-only, backup naming, restore, hand-edit state, rebuild, exports); `checks_spec_editor` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (1100x700: edit a cell, pending panel, red tag + icon + tooltip, dialog blocks without a reason, history entry; screenshots with SHOT_DIR).
- UNVERIFIED / not done: the cell highlight is overlay labels, only seen on Xvfb Tk 9 (never Windows / Segoe UI); `problems` (stored sheet issues) are not recomputed after an edit; `params` (raw parse) is not updated by span / frequency edits; the Notes tab and JSON editing of qualifiers / raw fields are lightly tested; PDF layout seen only as a file, not in a viewer; the Standards Specs model itself stays read-only.

- Tests: `python -m unittest tests.test_spec_editor` (54, no Tk: typed values, byte-identical round trip of all 11 sheets, diff / revert, per-row issue list, atomic write with failure injection, log failure, append-only, backup naming, restore, hand-edit state, rebuild, exports); `checks_spec_editor` in `tests/real_tk_checks.py` (1100x700: edit a cell, pending panel, red tag + icon + tooltip, dialog blocks without a reason, history entry; screenshots with SHOT_DIR).
- UNVERIFIED / not done: the cell highlight is overlay labels, only seen on Xvfb Tk 9 (never Windows / Segoe UI); the draft's 'Open source page...' is not implemented; `problems` (stored sheet issues) are not recomputed after an edit; `params` (raw parse) is not updated by span / frequency edits; an existing blocking error would block unrelated saves (shipped sheets have none); the Notes tab and JSON editing of qualifiers / raw fields are lightly tested; PDF layout seen only as a file, not in a viewer; the Standards Specs model itself stays read-only.

## Readiness check and stop-on-surprise (branch `readiness-check`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never run on hardware)

Why: every new automated procedure has needed several revisions on real hardware (8808A `=>` / `?>` prompt characters and `*TER` answering `?>`; DCI settle tunables not registered; the Datasheet Builder prompting for an unverified `ERR?`; a driver editing shared `drivers/transport.py` (in 40 sidecars) and flipping verified procedures; too many prompts in a fully automated procedure; points with no uncertainty). The two tools below find what the simulated bench can show before the owner does, and stop a real run at the first thing it did not expect. No new logging system: both read the existing `CommandLog` (the run log / transcript).

### 1. Readiness check (no hardware): `tools/readiness_check.py`

    python tools/readiness_check.py <procedure id | launcher | sidecar>  [--verbose] [--json out.json] [--no-run] [--strict] [--base REF] [--registry lab_registry.sqlite3]
    python tools/readiness_check.py --all [--json out.json]      one summary table, about 3 minutes

Manager: right-click a Procedures row, **Readiness check (no hardware)...** (runs the same tool in a subprocess and shows the report; `procedure_menu.READINESS`, `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.readiness_check`; the whole Manager diff is that menu item and one method). Exit code 1 only for a FAIL (`--strict`: WARN too).

It runs the procedure's whole flow once on the virtual bench of `--demo` (`tools/readiness_run.py` in a fresh interpreter with a throw-away HOME and tkinter replaced by the test stub, so dialogs are recorded and answered, never shown), then reads the command log and the dialogs. WaveSurfer (canned-data demo) and the three guided adjustments (the demo issues no CAL commands on purpose) get the static checks only. One procedure takes 1 to 10 s (MDO4000C about 10 s).

| check | what it reports | level |
|---|---|---|
| commands | every command sent, with its COMMANDS verified flag and manual page; unverified ones listed; wire text sent without a registry entry (cannot carry a flag or be overridden); `*IDN?` exempt | unverified commands in use: INFO note (owner decision; the list is still printed, for the final summary; exit codes and `--strict` unchanged); WARN for wire text with no registry entry or a verified command with no page; PASS otherwise |
| tunables | `pm_param` names (static + asked at run time) not registered in `procmods_engine`, a different default, an unknown engine prefix (the 8808A DCI settle case) | FAIL |
| prompts | operator prompts per measured point; hook-up / safety / setup prompts are the procedure; flagged: the same prompt 3 or more times (high-voltage questions differ by voltage), or more than 0.8 per point (and 12 or more) when the procedure reads its own meter (TDS2000C, verified, is 0.71). Typed-reading procedures are exempt | WARN |
| uncertainty | points that should print an uncertainty (Datasheet Builder rule 5, `tartur.NATURAL_UNITS`, `no_uncertainty`, same functions the report uses) and print none: inside the scope of accreditation = FAIL, scope not covering it = INFO (WARN when 5 or more or 10 percent), coverage unknown = WARN | FAIL / WARN / INFO |
| readback | a registered write whose declared read-back query is not sent within the next 120 commands of that instrument (the comparison itself is in the engine's code and is not visible here; that is `surprise.py`); output-enable without OPER? / OUTP? is INFO | WARN |
| outputs | an output ON at the end of the run = FAIL; switched on again after a recorded point without standby / off = WARN (a sweep inside one point is normal) | FAIL / WARN |
| housekeeping | an unverified err / esr / stb / opc / status query that the dry run sent and that is not on the Datasheet Builder HOUSEKEEPING list (it would prompt) | WARN |
| diagrams | hook-up picture names the run looked for (`hookups.py`) that are not in `diagrams/`, and prefixes with no picture at all | WARN |
| fingerprint | sidecar `fingerprint.code` / `.spec` against the files the run really used (driver MRO, engine imports, the procedure's own spec); shared files listed (INFO) | WARN when incomplete |
| shared | files changed on the branch against `origin/main` (merge-base) plus uncommitted, which fingerprints they sit in, which procedures flip to "Changed since verification", and whether one of the verified ones does (seeded list, or `--registry` for the real one) | FAIL if a verified one flips, WARN otherwise |
| surprise | a NEW procedure (not on the base) whose driver classes do not inherit `surprise.SurpriseMixin` | WARN |
| sidecar | the sidecar parses and names files that exist | FAIL |

Calibration (so it does not flood): a finding that is true for most existing procedures is INFO. Details of INFO lines are hidden unless `--verbose`. Thresholds are constants at the top of the file.

Findings over every registered procedure (`--all`, 40 sidecars, 0 FAIL): all of them had the same finding, **unverified commands in use** (now an INFO note, no longer a WARN; handheld meters 2 to 7, 8808A 4, truevolt 5, 34970A / 72A 10, 34410A / 11A 23, K2000 37, MDO4000C 47, the three generators 33 to 34 of 36). Beyond that:

| finding | procedures |
|---|---|
| hook-up pictures missing from `diagrams/` | 8808A (9 files, e.g. `hook_8808A_5522A_VOLT.png`), 34970A / 34972A (10 each), 34410A (10) / 34411A (11), K2000 verification (7) and adjustment (1), Fluke 365 / 374 / 375 / 376 / 376-II (2 each), TDS2000C (1), 33210A / 33220A / 33250A (no `hook_332xx*` picture at all) |
| sidecar fingerprint list incomplete | 34460A / 61A / 65A / 70A: `drivers/scpi_dmm.py` (class used in the run) is not listed; 33210A / 33220A / 33250A: `specs/fgen_332xx_reference_cmc.json` is read by the run but not in the spec fingerprint |
| same prompt shown more than once | Agilent 34401A (the OHM2 / OHM4 rewire prompt 7x each), 8808A (the 10 A terminal prompt 3x) |
| raw wire text not in any COMMANDS registry | TDS2000C (89 kinds) |

Not fixed here (existing procedures are untouched). Not covered: whether a reply FORMAT is what the real instrument sends, settle times, front-panel steps, prompt characters (only the instrument tells; see the questionnaire), WaveSurfer and the adjustments (static only). Tests: `tests/test_readiness_check.py` (synthetic transcripts for each check, exit codes, one simulated run, hardware libraries blocked).

### 2. Stop on surprise: `surprise.py`

A procedure runs at full speed and pauses ONLY on (a) a reply not in the expected format (empty, timeout, a prompt character `=>` `?>` `!>` where a value was expected), (b) a write whose read-back does not match what was set, (c) a non-empty error queue after a write. ONE dialog (command as logged, reply, expected, the last 20 log lines) with **Stop** / **Continue**; closing it is Stop; the default button is Stop. Stop raises `SurpriseStop` (an `InstrumentError`), so each engine's existing handler puts the instruments in standby / off and shows its error. Continue is logged (`!! SURPRISE ...`, `!! SURPRISE: operator chose Continue`). A clean run never asks.

Rules kept: a write is sent once and never retried (`set_checked` = send, read back, compare, then the error queue); only a read-only query that timed out is retried, once. Nothing retries automatically after a surprise. Passwords stay masked (the dialog shows the logged form). Without a handler (headless) a surprise is a Stop. The worker thread never calls Tk: it queues the request and a poller started by `surprise.tk_handler(root)` ON the Tk thread (`root.after`, 50 ms) shows the dialog, so make the handler where the Run button's handler runs, before the worker starts; if nothing picks the request up within 5 s (no mainloop, handler made on a worker thread) or the dialog cannot be built, the answer is Stop, never a hang (a first version called `root.after` from the worker and hung on a Python 3.12 / Tk 9 build in this sandbox, found by the real-Tk test). **Demo mode is unaffected**: on a virtual command log the guard is off (`enable_surprise` / `attach_guard` do nothing) unless `force=True` (tests only).

Survey result: today each driver compares its own read-back and raises `InstrumentError`; the engines' worker threads catch `Exception`, call `standby()` and show an error dialog (stop only: no Continue, no last-lines view); error queues are drained at reset (`Instrument._reset_drain`) and read in a few drivers after a write (`err.query` in keysight_dacq, keysight_332xx, keysight_5313x, fluke_8808a via `*ESR?` bits). No single shared hook exists without editing `drivers/base.py` (`send` / `ask` / `query`), which is in all 40 sidecars.

How it is wired (opt-in, no shared file edited):
- **New drivers** (default): `drivers/_template.py` now declares `class ModelName(SurpriseMixin, Instrument)` and its example setter is `return self.set_checked('something.set', value)` (the read-back command is the one named in `Cmd.readback`); reads use `ask_checked(key, expect='float'|'int'|'any'|regex)`; `errq_checkpoint()` reads `err.query` once (set `ERR_CLEAN` on the class if the empty reply is not `0,"No error"`). The engine of a new procedure calls `inst.enable_surprise(surprise.tk_handler(root))` after connecting. The readiness check warns when a new procedure's driver lacks the mixin.
- **Existing procedures**: not wired on `readiness-check` (the engines wired afterwards are listed in 2b below). `surprise.attach_guard(instrument, handler)` wraps one instrument OBJECT (empty reply / prompt character on `ask`) without touching its class, but calling it needs an edit in an engine or driver file. Blast radius of wiring, from the sidecar fingerprints in this tree (each file listed: those procedures flip to "Changed since verification"; seeded verified: Fluke 117, TDS2000C, WaveSurfer):

| where the edit would go | procedures that flip | seeded verified that flip |
|---|---|---|
| `drivers/base.py` or `drivers/transport.py` (the one shared hook) | all 40 | all three |
| `drivers/fluke_5560a.py` (calibrator driver) | 36 | all three |
| `handheld_dmm.py` (all handheld engines) | 19: 87V, 110, 113, 114, 115, 116, 117, Klein MM325, 175, 177, 179, 287, 289, 77IV, 365, 374, 375, 376, 376-II | Fluke 117 |
| `truevolt_verification.py` (+ `drivers/keysight_truevolt.py`) | 4: 34460A, 34461A, 34465A, 34470A | none |
| `fgen_verification.py` (wired, 2b) | 3: 33210A, 33220A, 33250A (`drivers/reference_instruments.py` also adds the MDO4000C) | none |
| `dacq_verification.py` | 2: 34970A, 34972A | none |
| `agilent34410a_verification.py` | 2: 34410A, 34411A | none |
| `drivers/scpi_dmm.py` | 3: 34401A, 8846A verification and adjustment | none |
| one engine / launcher only (`fluke8808a_verification.py`, `mdo4000c_verification.py`, `keithley2000_verification.py`, `tds2000c_verification.py`, `Procedures/<launcher>.py`) | 1 each | TDS2000C if `tds2000c_verification.py` |
| the new `surprise.py` / `drivers/_template.py` / `tools/` / `tests/` | 0 (in no fingerprint) | none |

Recommendation (the flip decision is the owner's): wire the unverified procedures first, one engine per branch (`truevolt_verification.py`, `fgen_verification.py`, `dacq_verification.py`, `agilent34410a_verification.py`: 11 procedures, none verified), and leave Fluke 117 / TDS2000C / WaveSurfer until they are re-verified anyway. Never wire through `drivers/base.py`.

Tests: `tests/test_surprise.py` (fault injection with a scripted instrument: bad reply, empty, prompt character, mismatched read-back, error-queue entry, timeout; no write repeated; clean run asks nothing; demo unaffected; masked password; shared files do not import `surprise`), `tests/real_tk_surprise.py` (real Tk 9 under Xvfb with a worker thread and `mainloop`: Continue, Stop, window close, text of the dialog, 20-line tail, no dialog on a clean run; run by `tests.test_surprise.RealTkDialog`; it needs a Python with tkinter, e.g. the uv 3.13), `tests/real_tk_checks.py::checks_manager_row_menu` (the menu item).

#### 2b. Existing engines wired for stop-on-surprise (branch `surprise-wiring`; simulated bench and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only, never on hardware)

Owner decision: wire the unverified engines one at a time, from the ENGINE file only (no driver, `drivers/base.py`, `drivers/transport.py` or `handheld_dmm.py` edit). Done so far: `truevolt_verification.py` (4 procedures), `dacq_verification.py` (2), `agilent34410a_verification.py` (2: 34410A, 34411A), `fgen_verification.py` (3: 33210A, 33220A, 33250A; the MDO4000C shares `drivers/reference_instruments.py` but is a different engine and is NOT wired). Nothing is left open in this list except the engines named in the blast-radius table that were deliberately not wired (handheld engines, 34401A / 8846A, 8808A, K2000, MDO4000C, TDS2000C, WaveSurfer).

Sidecars: `surprise.py` is in the fingerprint file list of every wired procedure (placed right after the engine; owner-approved; the 8 Truevolt / dacq / 34410A procedures first, the three generators with their own wiring). It flipped nothing that was not already flipped by the wiring itself (checked with a throw-away registry: every procedure verified at the commit before the wiring, status read after). `tools/readiness_check.py` expects `surprise.py` in the list whenever an engine or driver of the procedure imports it, and reports it as listed once it is (a wired procedure without it gets the 'incomplete' WARN).

What the engine-level wiring is (the same in every wired engine):
- `IO.__init__(..., surprise_handler=None)` and `IO.enable_surprise(handler, force=False)`: `surprise.attach_guard` on each instrument OBJECT (class untouched) with `expect={'read': 'float'}` and `stop_only=True`, so an empty reply / a prompt character (`=>` `?>` `!>`) / a non-number where a reading is expected / a query that timed out twice is a surprise; a query that times out ONCE is retried once (read-only, the standing rule). `enable_surprise` returns `[]` on a demo (virtual) command log, so the simulated bench never prompts; `force=True` is for tests only.
- `App._surprise_handler()` makes `surprise.tk_handler(self.root)` on the Tk thread (the Test connections / Run handler, once per window) and gives it to the real `IO`; it returns None in every demo mode (virtual and fast), so a demo never builds a dialog.
- The error-queue checks the engine already makes after a write go through `surprise.checked(instrument, driver_check, ...)`: with no guard that is exactly the driver call as before (same commands, same exception); with a guard the driver's own `InstrumentError` ("queue not empty") becomes the ONE Stop / Continue dialog (command as logged, reply, the last 20 log lines). The driver still decides what is an error; nothing is copied. `SafetyError` (a safe state not confirmed) and `SurpriseStop` pass through untouched.
- Stop raises `SurpriseStop` (an `InstrumentError`), so the engine's existing worker handler runs: outputs to standby (`io.standby()`), the engine's error dialog, `busy` cleared; the points before it stay recorded. Continue returns and the point goes on / is recorded exactly as it always is; `!! SURPRISE ...` and `!! SURPRISE: operator chose Continue` are in the command log (the run log saved with the session). Nothing is recorded differently, no pass / fail logic changed.
- A surprise is **Stop only** (no Continue button; the guard stops whatever the handler answers: `Surprise.stop_only`) where going on would use a reply that is not there, or would enable an output after a refusal. Everything else offers Continue.
- A write is never retried (the guard only re-asks a read-only query after a timeout).

Truevolt (34460A / 34461A / 34465A / 34470A):
| surprise | where | dialog |
|---|---|---|
| empty / prompt character / non-number reply to `READ?`; any reply problem on the DMM or the 5560A (`ROUT:TERM?`, `OPER?`, `ERR?` ...); a timeout twice | every `ask` of both instruments | Stop only |
| DMM error queue not empty after `CONF` / `NPLC` / `BAND` (`dmm.check_errors`) | `IO.measure` | Stop / Continue |
| 5560A error queue not empty after `OUT` (`cal.check_error`) | `IO.measure` | Stop only: the calibrator refused the setting and the next step is `OPER` |
| read-back mismatch | none in this engine's point path (writes are verified through the error queue). The only confirmations are the calibrator's `STBY` / `OPER?` states, which stay a `SafetyError` / `InstrumentError` hard stop (reset fails, outputs to standby), never a Continue question | n/a |

34970A / 34972A (`dacq_verification.py`, 5560A / 5522A / 5520A as the source): same wiring (`IO.enable_surprise`, `App._surprise_handler`).
| surprise | where | dialog |
|---|---|---|
| empty / prompt character / non-number reply to `READ?`; any reply problem on the DMM or the calibrator; a timeout twice | every `ask` of both instruments | Stop only |
| DMM error queue not empty after `CONF` / `NPLC` / `BAND` / `ROUT:SCAN` (`dmm.check_errors`, up to 5 entries; the dialog lists the entries, not the closing 'No error') | `IO.measure`, before the output | Stop / Continue |
| DMM error queue not empty after the reading (the output is already in standby) | `IO.measure`, after `READ?` and `STBY` (also the zero-offset points) | Stop / Continue: the reading is kept and recorded as usual |
| calibrator error queue not empty after `OUT` (`cal.check_error`) | `IO.measure` | Stop only (never `OPER` after a refusal) |
| read-back mismatch | none in the point path (`configure_point` only sends); the calibrator `STBY` / `OPER?` confirmation stays a hard stop | n/a |

34410A / 34411A (`agilent34410a_verification.py`, 5560A / 5522A / 5520A as the source; the one engine with real read-backs): same wiring.
| surprise | where | dialog |
|---|---|---|
| empty / prompt character / non-number reply to `READ?`; any reply problem on the meter or the calibrator; a timeout twice | every `ask` of both instruments (the meter's `check_errors` uses `query_retry`, which does not pass through `ask`: an error queue that answers nothing at all is caught by `surprise.checked`, shown as a bad reply) | Stop only |
| read-back mismatch of the meter's configuration: function, range, autorange off, NPLC / AC filter, gate time, input range (`dmm.configure_point`, `Keysight34410A._confirm`) | `IO.measure` | Stop only: the driver call fails part-way, so the rest of the configuration was never sent; the write is not repeated |
| read-back mismatch of the 5522A's `ZCOMP` (`cal.set_compensation`, resistance points) | `IO.compensation` | Stop only (the 5560A / 5520A have no such command) |
| meter error queue not empty after the configuration, and after the reading (the output is still on until the engine's own `finally` puts it in standby) | `IO.measure` | Stop / Continue: the reading is kept and recorded as usual |
| calibrator error queue not empty after `OUT` (`cal.check_error`) | `IO.measure` | Stop only (never `OPER` after a refusal) |
Stop here also ends in `STBY` twice (the engine's `finally` and its worker's `except` block, both existing code). The Front/Rear switch mismatch (`check_terminals`) is an instruction to the operator, not a surprise, and is unchanged. An overload reading (9.9E37, a valid number) still raises the driver's own 'overload / invalid reading' error.

33210A / 33220A / 33250A (`fgen_verification.py`; the generator under test, the 5790B, and the counter / power meter when they have an address): same wiring (`IO.enable_surprise`, `App._surprise_handler` made in Test connections, never in demo). Differences from the multimeter engines, all forced by the drivers: the settings are driver methods that set, read back, compare AND read the error queue in one call (`Keysight332xx.setup`, `Fluke5790B.select_input` / `set_external_trigger` / `set_range_for`, `Keysight5313x.set_reference` / `configure_frequency`), so those calls go through `IO._checked` = `surprise.checked(..., classify=...)`: the engine says from the exchange the call made whether the failure was the read-back (command is a query other than ERR?) or the error queue (command is `ERR?`).
| surprise | where | dialog |
|---|---|---|
| empty / prompt character / non-number reply (`FREQ?`, `VOLT?`, `OFFS?`, `OUTP:LOAD?`, `*TST?`, `MEAS?`, `RANGE?`, `EXTRIG?`, `READ?`, the counter's setting queries, `MEASure:POWer?` ...); any reply problem on any instrument; a timeout twice | every `ask` of the generator (except `OUTP?`), the 5790B, the counter, the power meter | Stop only |
| read-back mismatch of a generator setting (termination, function, offset, autorange, unit, frequency, amplitude) | `IO._out` (`uut.setup`) | Stop only: the driver call stops part-way, the amplitude and the output were never sent; no write is repeated |
| read-back mismatch of the 5790B input / EXTRIG / RANGE, of the counter's function / impedance / coupling / arms / gate / expected frequency / reference | `IO._measure`, `IO._flat_wideband`, `IO._frequency` | Stop only |
| generator `OUTP?` does not say ON after `OUTP ON` (a reply that is a number but not 1, or empty) | `IO._out` (`uut.output(True)`) | Stop only |
| generator error queue after the setting (`SYST:ERR?` at the end of `setup`) | `IO._out` | Stop only: the output is enabled next, never after a refusal |
| 5790B error queue after `INPUT` / `RANGE`, counter error queue after its setting, generator error queue after the self-test | `IO._measure`, `_frequency` | Stop only: the driver method does more after the queue check (sets `last_input`, returns the range, sets `gate_s`, returns the self-test result), so Continue would leave it half done |
| 5790B error queue after `EXTRIG` | `IO._measure`, `IO._flat_wideband` | Stop / Continue: the queue check is the last thing `set_external_trigger` does, so Continue leaves it exactly as if the queue had been empty |
Not surprises (the driver's own message is kept, no dialog): a setting refused before anything is sent (e.g. a sine above the model's range), the counter's `+2009` 'no valid external timebase' (an expected diagnosis with its own instructions), an invalid 5790B reading code, no valid frequency / power reading, a flatness reference far from the expected level, and the counter's error queue after a READ? (all are existing driver / engine errors with a specific message). The generator's `OUTP?` is NOT guarded (`attach_guard(skip=('outp.query',))`): an output state it cannot confirm stays a `SafetyError` ('check the front panel', title SAFETY) or the driver's 'did not confirm output ON', also in `all_off` and `cleanup()`, never a dialog. `_std_volts` and `_power` pass a `SurpriseStop` through instead of wrapping it in their 'check the cable' message (the dialog already said what and where). Stop goes through `measure()`'s own `finally: all_off()` (OUTP OFF, OUTP?), then the engine's `_error` dialog. `surprise.py` gained two backward-compatible options for this: `attach_guard(skip=...)` and `checked(classify=...)`.

Tests: `tests/test_surprise_wiring.py` (per engine: every fault type injected into a simulated transport with `tests/surprise_faults.py`; exactly one dialog; Stop-only where stated; no write repeated; Stop ends in `STBY` (generators: `OUTP OFF`); Continue records the same results as a clean run; a clean run asks nothing and gives the same command log, results, report rows and PDF bytes with and without the guard; demo never builds a dialog; a real connection wires the guard with the window's handler) and `tests/real_tk_surprise_engine.py` (the real engine window on a real Tk 9 under Xvfb: a worker thread meets the fault, the dialog appears on the Tk thread with the right buttons, Stop / Continue do what is promised). Found: on the sandbox's uv Python 3.12 / Tk 9.0.3 a worker thread's `root.after(...)` never runs (the engines' own `work()` threads do this), so the real-Tk engine test uses the newest Python with tkinter (3.13); not specific to the wiring.

### 3. New-procedure checklist (suggested text for the new-procedure skill)

1. Intake: manual pages for every command; run the questionnaire below BEFORE writing code.
2. Drivers inherit `SurpriseMixin`; every set goes through `set_checked` with `Cmd.readback`; every query through `ask_checked(expect=...)`; never an f-string command; `verified=True` only with a page in `ref`; do not edit `drivers/base.py`, `drivers/transport.py`, `handheld_dmm.py` or another engine (list any shared edit in the report and ask first).
3. Register every `pm_param` name with `_t(...)` in `procmods_engine` (same default) and give the engine prefix a `MODEL_IDS` entry.
4. Housekeeping queries (`SYST:ERR?`, `*ESR?`, `*OPC?`, `*STB?`) go through the registry using the standard headers so the Datasheet Builder does not prompt; if the model's header differs, say so and ask.
5. Prompts: hook-up, safety and set-up only; none per point in an automated procedure; one picture name per hook-up in `hookups.py` (the readiness check lists missing `diagrams/` names; the owner supplies the PNGs).
6. Every point that the lab's scope of accreditation covers prints an uncertainty (and TAR / TUR where there is a specification row).
7. Outputs to standby / off after each point; `cleanup()` never raises.
8. Sidecar: list the engine, every driver in the MRO (and `surprise.py` if a driver imports it), the spec and `hookups.py`; run `python tools/readiness_check.py <id>` until it has no FAIL and every WARN is explained in the final summary; make sure the shared-file check says no existing procedure flips.
9. Final summary: UNVERIFIED commands and limits (from the readiness report), shared-file touches, missing pictures, what was not run on hardware.

Owner questionnaire at build time (ask before coding; answers go in the driver docstring and the spec):
- Interface and wire: the exact prompt character(s) the instrument returns (`=>` `?>` `!>` or none), command and reply terminators, echo on or off, baud / parity for RS-232, what `*IDN?` returns verbatim.
- Does it answer `*OPC?`, `*ESR?`, `SYST:ERR?` (or what is the error query)? What does an empty error queue look like verbatim?
- Settle times seen on the bench per function / range (the first reading after a range change, DCI, AC, resistance, frequency), and whether the reading keeps drifting for a minute.
- Front-panel steps the operator must do that remote control cannot (terminal selection, current jack, filters, local lockout, self-cal, warm-up time) and which of them are safety relevant.
- Error behaviour: what the instrument does on a bad command (beep, error queue entry, hangs, no reply), whether a reply can be late, whether it needs a delay after `*RST` or after a range change.
- What the owner has seen go wrong on the previous model of the same family.

### Unverified in this branch

- Every reply format and prompt character the checks assume is generic (`=>` `?>` `!>`, numeric replies, `0,"No error"` / `+0,"No error"` for an empty queue); no real instrument was available. Drivers with another format must set `ERR_CLEAN` or `expect`.
- The Manager menu item runs the tool with `sys.executable`; a frozen build without a Python interpreter shows the "not found" message (not tried on Windows).
- The real-Tk dialog was seen under Xvfb with Tk 9 only, not on Windows.
- `--all` takes about 3 minutes; the MDO4000C run is the longest.


## Review window before the PDF (branch pdf-review-window)
Export Report (PDF) now opens a REVIEW window instead of the details popup: every test point with its assigned uncertainty (step, test point, tolerance, standard used, budget, scope minimum, reported k=2, TUR, status), the report details in a compact strip (technician, temperature, humidity, default standard, TAR / TUR tick boxes, 'Standards used...').
- Files: `report_review_model.py` (Tk-free: `PointSpec`, `evaluate`, `Row`, `ReviewModel`, `specs_for(app)`, `review_block`, `parse_uncertainty` / `format_manual`), `report_review.py` (the window `ReviewWindow` and `ask(parent, procedure)`), `report_dialog.ask_details` (same name and signature; calls `report_review.ask`, falls back to `ask_details_plain` = the old popup; `report_dialog.USE_REVIEW = False` switches the window off), `report_pdf.apply_uncertainty_review` (called first thing in `write_calibration_pdf`), `run_queue_engine.queue_export_pdfs` (sets `_review_all_meters` around the dialog call: one review per meter in sequence).
- ZERO FLIPS: no engine file and no launcher is edited. None of `report_review*.py`, `report_dialog.py`, `report_pdf.py`, `report_snapshot.py`, `run_queue_engine.py` is in any `Procedures/*.procedure.json` fingerprint list or in `verified.USER_CODE`, so no procedure (verified or not, built-in or user-built) shows 'Changed since verification' because of this branch (checked: the fingerprint files of all 40 sidecars are byte-identical to origin/main; `tests/test_reprint_review.py::Wiring`). Keep it that way: do not add a hook line to an engine without agreeing which procedures flip.
- How the window finds the engine without a hook: `report_review.find_app(parent)` scans for the one object whose `.root is parent` and which has `certificate_rows_and_points()`; it builds the same rows and points the engine is about to print. Anything else (no App found, an engine without `certificate_rows_and_points`, any error) returns `UNAVAILABLE` and the old popup appears: nothing breaks.
- Coverage. PHASE 1 (window shown): handheld_dmm (all 19 handhelds, run queue, user-built datasheets / Datasheet Builder runs), truevolt (34460A-70A), 8846A, 34401A, dacq (34970A / 34972A), 8808A, keithley2000, agilent34410a (34410A / 34411A). PHASE 2 (old popup still shown, these have only `certificate_pairs()` / own export code and uncertainties that are not calibrator budgets): fgen (33210A / 33220A / 33250A: 5790B measuring standard + reference CMC file), MDO4000C, TDS2000C (DC scope CMC rows), WaveSurfer (three call sites, `sources` instead of points). They can join without an engine edit by giving the model a `specs_for` adapter (read-only rows for measuring-standard / CMC-only points) plus a `certificate_pairs` branch in `find_app`.
- Numbers: `report_pdf.point_uncertainty(point, standard)` (printed Uncertainty = max(budget, scope CMC) rounded UP to 2 s.f., k = 2), `tartur.row_fields` (TAR / TUR), `uncertainty_budget.build` (budget `.emu`, scope minimum `.cmc`). An untouched row keeps exactly the strings the engine built (`apply_uncertainty_review` only touches rows listed in `changes`; a review with no change prints a PDF identical to before: tested). Reported can never be below the scope minimum (tested for every point x every offered standard; `evaluate` also clamps).
- Standard choice: the drop-down offers the calibrators of `report_review_model.SOURCE_STANDARDS` (5560A, 5522A, 5520A) whose published sheet has a row covering the point (`offered_standards`). Locked (no drop-down, greyed): zero-offset / no-source points, rows with no calibrator, rows where no uncertainty is printed today (also the 8808A hand-measured points), frequency rows (Frequency - Measure CMC alone, shown as 'Lab frequency'), measuring-standard rows. 'Set standard for selected rows' offers the standards every selected row can use; the 'Default standard' box in the strip moves every row that has no choice of its own. No reason is asked for a change.
- Status: As budgeted / Raised to scope (budget < scope minimum: the scope minimum is reported) / Not in scope (the scope has no entry: red; prints what the engine prints today) / Standard changed / Manual (not in scope). Filters All / Needs attention (Not in scope + Manual only; owner answer: 'Raised to scope' is routine and not listed) / Changed by me.
- Typed uncertainty (owner request): ONLY a 'Not in scope' row has an editable Reported cell (click it; value + unit, e.g. `3.5 mV`, `2 uV`, `1.2e-3 V`; a bare number is in the unit named in the hint; parsed with Decimal; must be > 0). It is printed as typed (the typed figures, SI scaling and spelling of `uncertainty.format_value`, NOT rounded up). TUR = tolerance / typed value, TAR blank. Status 'Manual (not in scope)', amber / red. Empty cell = back to the engine's value. Rows that are in scope cannot be typed into (they use a standard change), so Reported cannot go below the scope minimum. Stored: value, typed text, who (the Technician field) and when (ISO time) in the change record.
- Persistence / Regenerate PDF: nothing new in the database (no schema change; `calibration_procedures.sqlite3` not touched). The review goes into `details['uncertainty_review']` = `{'version': 1, 'reviews': [{asset, procedure, default_standard, engine_default, changed, manual, changes[], table[]}]}`; `details` is part of the report snapshot (`report_snapshots.payload_json`, SHA-256), and the snapshot also holds the printed rows (already with the new Uncertainty / TAR / TUR). Regenerate PDF prints those stored rows and re-applies the same review (idempotent; an index is only trusted when Description and Nominal match). `changes[]` per row: idx, step, section, description, nominal, original_standard, standard, uncertainty, tar, tur, budget, scope_minimum, reported, unit, status, manual (+ manual_value, manual_text, entered_by, entered_at). `table[]` is the whole grid as shown (the audit record of what the technician saw). Older snapshots (no key) regenerate as before.
- Demo: the window opens in demo runs too ('DEMO DATA - NOT CALIBRATION EVIDENCE' notice, the popup title carries the demo suffix); the PDF keeps its DEMO marking; a demo report is never snapshotted, so a demo review is never recorded.
- Defaults chosen for the owner's open questions (each a one-line change): not-in-scope rows print what the engine prints (`Row.reported_text`); the drop-down offers `SOURCE_STANDARDS`; no new PDF column or marker (the standard change shows only through the numbers and the stored record); queue = one review per meter (`run_queue_engine.queue_export_pdfs`); no new table; rows with no calibrator are locked; TAR / TUR tick boxes live in the strip. 'Needs attention' counts every 'Raised to scope' row (most rows, because the lab CMC is usually above the 5560A / 5522A budget).
- Tests: `tests/test_reprint_review.py` (model: rows from the real 34470A run on the simulated bench and the handheld run on the simulated calibrators equal what the engines print; standard change hand-computed from the sheet; never below the scope minimum; locked rows; bulk / default / reset / filters; typed uncertainty parse / print / clear / in-scope refusal; PDF + snapshot + Regenerate PDF round trip with page-identical reprint; demo marking; zero-flip wiring), `tests/test_reprint_review_queue.py` (one review per meter on the real queue app), `tests/real_tk_review.py <light|dark> <dir> [demo]` (real Tk under Xvfb at 1360x860 and 1000x640: opens, fits, drives selection / bulk / per-row drop-down / filters / typed uncertainty / reset / Generate, saves screenshots; not a unittest module). Run only these (and `test_reprint_roundtrip`, `test_reprint_snapshot`, `test_reprint_ui`, `test_tartur_*`, `test_beta_run_queue`); `tests/test_shell` hangs on main.
- UNVERIFIED: the window on Windows (fonts, DPI, the Windows combobox pop-down); only Xvfb with Tk 9.0 and bitmap fonts was seen. The per-row drop-down is an overlay combobox opened by a click on the Standard cell (the grid is a Treeview, not 100+ embedded widgets). 5522A / 5520A budgets for rows the engine printed with the 5560A are the published-sheet numbers already in the suite (no new limit or sheet). Phase 2 engines not covered. The 'Standards used...' sub-dialog is the old three-row table (description, serial, due).
- Backups of the changed files before this work: `_backup_v01/` (git-ignored): `report_pdf.py`, `report_dialog.py`, `run_queue_engine.py`, `NOTES.md`.

## Review window, phase 2: generators and oscilloscopes (branch pdf-review-phase2)
The review window now also opens for fgen (33210A / 33220A / 33250A), MDO4000C, TDS2000C and WaveSurfer 4104HD. ZERO engine, launcher or sidecar edits: 0 of 40 procedures change fingerprint (`flips.py` old/new tree compare; and a registry with every procedure verified on origin/main shows 0 not-verified on this branch, including the seeded Fluke 117, TDS2000C and WaveSurfer). Edited shared modules only: `report_review.py`, `report_review_model.py`, `report_pdf.py`; new `report_review_adapters.py` (none in any fingerprint list or `verified.USER_CODE`).
- Discovery: `report_review.COVERED` = `certificate_rows_and_points` (phase 1), `certificate_pairs` (fgen / MDO / TDS), `certificate_source_records` (WaveSurfer); `find_app` still needs exactly one object with `.root is parent`. `report_review_model.specs_for` hands engines without `certificate_rows_and_points` to `report_review_adapters.specs_for` (WaveSurfer by `scope_uncertainty_value`; otherwise fgen by module `measure_budget`, MDO by `CAL_MODEL`, TDS by `uncertainty_parts`). `report_review.asset_of(app)` reads `asset` or WaveSurfer's `instrument_id` (this fell back silently to the old popup until the real-window check caught it).
- WaveSurfer: the three export routes ask before the rows exist for the per-tab `export_calibration_section(kind)`; `hint_kinds(app)` peeks (read-only) at that caller's `kind` local so the tab export reviews only that tab; the all-tabs export reviews every tab. If the peek finds nothing, all tabs are shown.
- PointSpec has a `calc(standard)` hook so the default standard returns the engine's OWN printed text, TAR and TUR (checked equal for every row of all four engines); another standard is recomputed from `uncertainty_budget.osc_dc(level, impedance, standard)` (the larger of the calibrator budget and the scope's oscilloscope DC CMC; MDO gain points combine the two levels in quadrature as % of the expected difference).
- Standard drop-down: only MDO4000C and WaveSurfer DC points that the engine budgets from the 5560A (gain, offset, DVM DC, trigger level); all three of 5560A / 5522A / 5520A are offered where the sheet covers the level. READ-ONLY (locked, 'Scope CMC' or the standard named): fgen (5790B measuring-standard budget, scope Measure CMC, `specs/fgen_332xx_reference_cmc.json`), TDS2000C (scope CMC only), and every scope / reference-CMC row of MDO and WaveSurfer. Manual entry applies only to Not-in-scope rows, in the row's unit (s, dB, div, V, Ohm, Hz...); `parse_uncertainty` / `format_manual` now also accept non-SI units (dB with prefixes).
- NO PDF layout change (owner): the review only replaces the Uncertainty (and TAR / TUR where those columns are on) text of rows listed in `changes`; it adds no column, marker, asterisk or footnote. `tests/test_reprint_review_phase2.py::Report` compares, for every engine, the PDF of a review with no change against the PDF without a review (page streams via `reprint_harness.differences`, extracted text, page count) and a changed review's marker-like tokens and page count against the baseline. The question of an asterisk / footnote for manual or out-of-scope uncertainties is therefore answered 'no' for now.
- `report_pdf.apply_uncertainty_review` now also accepts a change whose stored index no longer matches when exactly one row has the same Description and Nominal (still never by index alone); an index that matches nothing unique is ignored as before.
- Tests: `tests/test_reprint_review_phase2.py` (adapters recognised, one spec per row, default text equals the engine's printed text, locked rows, typing only on Not-in-scope rows, hand check of a 5522A DC budget against `osc_dc`, floor at the scope minimum, discovery through `report_dialog.ask_details` for each engine and the WaveSurfer per-tab hint, PDF + snapshot + Regenerate PDF round trip for MDO / WaveSurfer / fgen / TDS, no-layout-change, no engine file in the diff), `tests/real_tk_review_phase2.py <light|dark> <dir> [mdo,fgen,tds,ws]` (real Tk under Xvfb at 1360x860 and 1000x640, light and dark: 78 checks per run, screenshots).
- Not verified / for the owner: the number of Not-in-scope rows is large for fgen (RF flatness in dB: 42) and WaveSurfer (RMS noise: 36), because those rows 'expect an uncertainty' and the lab scope has no entry; MDO shows 25. Whether the simulated-bench numbers match the real instruments' reports (only simulated benches and sampled points were used); the stack peek for the WaveSurfer tab export (relies on the local name `kind` in `export_calibration_section`); Windows fonts / DPI (Xvfb Tk 9.0 only). The Omega sign did not render in the Xvfb bitmap font for some MDO descriptions ('50 , 20 MHz'); the report text itself is the engine's.
- Backups before this work: `_backup_v01/` (git-ignored): `report_pdf.py`, `report_review.py`, `report_review_model.py`, `report_dialog.py`, `NOTES.md`, `test_reprint_review.py`.

## MDO4000C RF level (absolute amplitude / flatness) rows take their uncertainty from the Fluke 96270A sheet (branch mdo-96270-uncertainty)
Owner decision: MDO4000C RF flatness rows (`ABSAMP`, dB, 15 points: +10 / 0 / -15 dBm x 50-950 kHz, 1-9 MHz, 10-90 MHz, 100 MHz-3 GHz, >3-6 GHz) get their uncertainty from the 96270A standard sheet, not the scope CMC (the lab scope has no RF level entry, so there is no floor). MDO4000C ONLY: fgen and every other engine are untouched; MDO crosstalk (`XTALK`), reference-frequency (`REFFREQ`), TPA-N-PRE (`TPAFUNC`) and IMD rows are exactly as before.
- Today the engine prints `N/A` for these rows (`uncertainty_parts` finds only `mdo4000c_reference_cmc.json`, all null); the review window showed them 'Not in scope'.
- Sheet rows used (specs/standards/fluke_96270a_standard.json, function 'RF Level Accuracy (into 50 ohm) [Leveling Head O/P]', rows 'down to -48 dBm', Technical Data 6002252B_EN p.6): 0-100 kHz +/-0.03 dB; 100 kHz-128 MHz +/-0.05 dB; 4 GHz +/-0.3 dB. `uncertainty_budget.rf_level(level_dbm, frequencies, standard='96270A', cmc=None)`: the sweep takes the LARGEST limit of the bands it crosses; u = limit / divisor (sheet level not printed: k = 2 assumed, as for every 96270A use); resolution only if the sheet row has one (none recorded); RSS; k = 2; reported = ceil_sig(max(EMU, CMC)) (CMC only if a caller passes one). dB natural units, no conversion; `NATURAL_UNITS` NOT changed (the adapter uses `tartur.from_values`, which only divides dB by dB).
- Result (all in dB): 50 kHz-950 kHz, 1-9 MHz, 10-90 MHz at +10 / 0 / -15 dBm (9 rows): budget 0.05, reported 0.050 dB, TAR = TUR = 1 dB / 0.05 dB = 20.0:1, status 'As budgeted', standard 96270A (the only drop-down entry). A sweep entirely below 100 kHz would be 0.03 dB.
- NOT covered (stay 'Not in scope', N/A, may take a typed value): 100 MHz-3 GHz and >3-6 GHz (6 rows): the sheet prints nothing between 128 MHz and 4 GHz and the only other rows are 'typical' (Microwave O/P Direct +/-0.5 dB) or the HF-option 'power flatness' points (100 MHz, 1 / 12 / 26.5 GHz), which are not used (typical, other output, no interpolation).
- PHASE NOISE (`PHASE`, 1 GHz carrier, 10 / 100 / 1000 kHz offset, dBc/Hz): the sheet has NO row. Note 8 only says 'Phase noise at 1 GHz: -144 dBc/Hz typical, 10 kHz to 100 kHz offset' (a typical figure of the source, not an uncertainty, nothing at 1000 kHz). Stopped there: the three rows stay 'Not in scope' / N/A; no number invented. The 96270A Extended Specifications (not supplied) would be needed.
- Where: `report_review_adapters.py` (`_rf_budget`, `_rf_level_spec`), `report_review_model.py` (`PointSpec.engine_printed`, `Row.applied`: a default that replaces what the engine prints is carried in `changes[]` with `original_standard` None; it is not 'changed by me'), `uncertainty_budget.py` (`rf_level`). The PDF and snapshot carry the value through the existing review block (`details['uncertainty_review']`, Regenerate PDF re-applies it). NO engine, sidecar, spec or launcher edit: 0 procedures flip. Consequence: a PDF made WITHOUT the review (the old-popup fallback) still prints N/A for these rows. Smallest engine edit if wanted (flips MDO4000C only): in `mdo4000c_verification.uncertainty_parts`, before the reference-file lookup, `if k == 'ABSAMP': b = report_review_adapters._rf_budget(p); return (b.reported, 'dB', 'si') if b else None` plus the same in `tar_tur_fields`.
- Tests: `tests/test_reprint_review_mdo_96270a.py` (hand bands, border, not-covered None, floor rules, review rows, phase / xtalk / reffreq / tpa untouched, PDF + snapshot + Regenerate, wiring); `tests/test_reprint_review_phase2.py` updated (MDO Not in scope 25 -> 22; the RF rows are the one intended difference of a no-change review).
- UNVERIFIED: every 96270A number above is the stored summary-table row (Technical Data p.6, summary specifications; the Extended Specifications were never supplied); confidence level / coverage factor not printed (k = 2 assumed); which 96270A output the lab uses for the sweep (Leveling Head rows assumed for <= 128 MHz); the sheet prints 0.001 dB level resolution (note 7) but it is not stored on the rows, so not in the budget (it would only move 0.050 to 0.051 dB through the round-up).

## UiPort and the Qt migration, Phase 1 foundation (branch `qt-phase1-foundation`, base `qt-migration` a56b1f2; simulated bench, stubbed Tk and a real Tk 9 / Python 3.12 under Xvfb only; never run on hardware, never on Windows)
Goal: later work swaps Tk for Qt (PySide6) WITHOUT editing the engines again. Engines stop importing tkinter and call a small interface, `UiPort`, which the host implements (Tk now, Qt later). No behaviour change on Tk: instrument commands, limits, pass/fail logic, safety-prompt TEXT and the order and number of prompts are untouched.
- New files: `ui_port.py` (the interface contract, `ScriptedUiPort`, `Answers`, the registry `get_ui()` / `set_ui()` and the proxy `ui_port.ui`; imports no toolkit), `ui_port_tk.py` (`TkUiPort`: the ONLY module of the port that imports tkinter; each method makes exactly the call the engine used to make), `agilent34410a_view_tk.py` (pilot: the engine's window code, moved). Tests / tools: `tests/ui_prompt_capture.py` + `tests/test_ui_prompts.py` + `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_*.json` (prompt sequences), `tools/ui_callsites.py` + `tests/test_ui_callsites.py` + `tests/golden_ui/callsites.json` (static prompt manifest of 18 files + 12 helpers), `tests/test_ui_port_clean.py` + `tests/ui_ported.txt` (which engines are ported), `tools/ui_flips.py` (fingerprint flips).
- Engines call it as `from ui_port import ui as port` (a proxy, resolved at call time, so `set_ui()` after import works). The host installs one implementation before any window exists; `TkUiPort` is created on first use if none was installed.

### What the existing golden transcripts do and do not lock
- `tests/golden/engine_*.json` (13 engines: 34401A, dacq 34970A/34972A, fgen 33220A, 8846A verification, handheld 5522A path, Keithley 2000, 34410A, 34411A, MDO4000C, TDS2000C, Truevolt 34470A, WaveSurfer DCV) lock the INSTRUMENT I/O of the IO classes only. None of them sees a dialog, a Toplevel or a thread hand-off. A port can leave them byte-identical and still change a prompt.
- Baseline before any edit (stubbed Tk, Python 3.13): `tests.test_golden_commands` (all driver + engine fixtures, 13 tests) OK; the engine / flow tests listed under 'Tests run' below OK. `tests/test_beta.py` cannot import here (needs a real tkinter), `tests/test_tartur_wiring_scopes.py::PdfExports::test_off_is_byte_identical_to_integration` fails with no change from this work: both fail identically on main.
- Files with NO engine golden transcript: Fluke 8808A verification (`fluke8808a_verification.py`; only driver fixtures), Keithley 2000 / Fluke 8808A / Fluke 8846A ADJUSTMENT launchers, the handheld families other than the 5522A path (`handheld_dmm.py` is only exercised through the 5522A fixture), `iflex_option.py`, `procspec.py`, `surprise.py` (has `tests/test_surprise*.py`). Added, additive only (no existing fixture touched):
  - `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_f8808a.json` (8808A: full runs on the 5522A and 5560A, HV refused, warm-up refused, the small dialogs), `ui_prompts_handheld.json` (virtual-bench runs of the 77 IV, 117 and 87V incl. the function-change cards and the reading windows), `ui_prompts_adjustments.json` (8808A and Keithley 2000 adjustment demo records: first steps then Abort), and the pilot's `ui_prompts_a34410a.json`. A record is `{kind, title, text, buttons, answer, ...}` (operator prompts also steps / facts / caution / image name / layout); the typed calibration code is masked. Fixtures live in `tests/golden_ui/`, NOT `tests/golden/`: `python -m tests.golden_capture --update` deletes every file in `tests/golden/` it does not know.
  - `tests/golden_ui/callsites.json`: for all 18 files and 12 shared helpers, every prompt call site as `(function, kind, title / text expressions, order)`, parsed with `ast`. The manifest is the same for a Tk call and for the equivalent port call, so a port must leave it byte-identical. This is the only characterization of the 8846A adjustment launcher (its App needs a serial meter; nothing headless drives it) and of `iflex_option.py` (one `askyesno`).
  - Gaps that remain: the 8846A adjustment window; the custom Toplevel dialogs (`ask_reading`, `ask_value`, the surprise dialog) are recorded as prompts but only at the Tk level; thread-timing behaviour is not characterized (the prompt ORDER is).
