## UiPort WP-G: function generators and scopes (branch `qt-wp-g-gen-scope`, base `qt-migration` fc528b7; simulated bench, stubbed Tk and a real Tk 9 under Xvfb only; never run on hardware)
- Ported (same recipe as the 34410A pilot; no new UiPort method was needed): `fgen_verification.py` (33220A, 33250A, 33210A) with `fgen_view_tk.py`, `mdo4000c_verification.py` with `mdo4000c_view_tk.py`, `tds2000c_verification.py` with `tds2000c_view_tk.py`. The window code (the theme / title / size statements, `_build_ui`, the busy-state button code, the results-table and address-box statements) moved unchanged into the views (`setup_window`, `finish_window`, `build_ui`, `sync_buttons`, `table_*`, `set_choices`); the engines keep one-line delegates. `tests/ui_ported.txt` lists the three (scenario keys `fgen`, `mdo4000c`, `tds2000c`).
- fgen: `ask_number_threadsafe` is now `port.call_from_worker(self.root, ask)` with `ask` calling `port.ask_number` (title, text, `initial=` unchanged). The worker's `root.after(0, ...)` are `port.post`, the between-points `after(30, ...)` are `port.after`.
- MDO4000C and TDS2000C: the engine's own request queue and 50 ms `_pump` are KEPT (`port.after(self.root, 50, self._pump)` and `port.operator_prompt` / `port.ask_text` inside it), NOT turned into `call_from_worker`: `request()` polls `self.stop` every 100 ms while it waits, so a Stop pressed while a prompt is open ends the run; `call_from_worker` blocks without that poll. Moving them to `call_from_worker` would change that behaviour (and MDO's `reconnect_scope` exemption), so it is left for the Qt side to decide.
- Prompt proof: `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_{fgen,mdo4000c,tds2000c}.json` were recorded on the UNPORTED engines (twice, `cmp` identical): fgen 33220A / 33250A / 33210A full virtual runs, test conditions and wiring prompts cancelled, typed readings cancelled, small dialogs, export / load cancelled; MDO4000C a full virtual run, the AFG option with no DMM / counter (typed readings through the manual dialog, a non-number once, all cancelled), first prompt cancelled, small dialogs, export / load; TDS2000C two models, first prompt cancelled, Read model, fast demo, small dialogs, export / load. Scenarios: `tests/ui_prompt_capture_wpg.py` (registered by two lines in `ui_prompt_capture.py`); Tk-level test `tests/test_ui_prompts_wpg.py`; the `ScriptedUiPort` run is `tests/test_ui_prompts.py::Port` (every line of `ui_ported.txt`). Both reproduce the fixtures byte for byte after the port.
- `call_from_worker` on a real Tk: `tests/real_tk_wpg_fgen.py` (run by `tests/test_wpg_real_tk.py` in a subprocess) runs two demo points of the real fgen window with a worker thread and mainloop; the worker asks for a typed counter reading; the real `simpledialog` window is answered / cancelled from the UI thread, and a third case has the dialog raise. Checked: the box is created on the Tk thread, the worker is alive and blocked while it is open, the point is recorded only after OK with exactly the typed value, Cancel and an exception both give the worker None and end the run with 'Reading not entered; run stopped.', the app's own handler reports the exception. The same script gives the same result on the UNPORTED engine (base fc528b7). On the uv Python 3.12 / Tk 9 build the run never starts, unported or ported (a worker's `root.after` never runs there, the quirk already noted in `test_surprise_wiring`); the test therefore takes the newest Python with tkinter (3.13).
- Real Tk widget tree of the three Apps (demo window built under Xvfb: class, text, state, style, values, width, wrap, grid / pack options, requested sizes, idle and busy button states, 54 / 496 / 24 table rows, detail text, title) is identical before and after the port.
- Fingerprints: the five sidecars (33220A, 33250A, 33210A, MDO4000C, TDS2000C) list the view, `ui_port.py` and `ui_port_tk.py` after the engine; `tools/ui_flips.py <base> .`: 0 flips before, exactly those 5 after. **Tektronix TDS2000C is `seed_verified: true` (verified on hardware): it now shows 'Changed since verification'; this needs the owner's sign-off.** Instrument commands, limits, pass / fail logic and every prompt text / order are untouched (`git diff tests/golden` empty).
- Odd / worth knowing: (1) the export check no longer imports `report_dialog` (the engines' `try: import report_pdf, report_dialog` is `try: import report_pdf`): the 'Report export' error fires only if `report_pdf` is missing, as in the pilot. (2) `tools/ui_callsites.py` now maps the port's `initial=` to the manifest's Tk spelling `initialvalue=` (the fixture was recorded before any port); `callsites.json` itself is unchanged. (3) Tests that patched `<engine>.filedialog` / `.messagebox` on these engines now patch tkinter's (`test_reprint_review_phase2.export`, `tartur_scopes_harness`); `F.messagebox = h.messagebox` assignments in other tests are harmless. (4) Not verified: Qt (no `_qt` view exists), Windows, hardware; `test_reprint_review_phase2` and `test_tartur_wiring_scopes::PdfExports::test_off_is_byte_identical_to_integration` fail identically on the base commit.

## Qt design system (qtui/) (branch `qt-p2-design`, Phase 2; look approved from the two prototypes; checked headless with PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never seen on Windows or a real screen; DARK ONLY for now)
Shared Qt Widgets building blocks and the app bootstrap that every later Qt screen reuses. Nothing here touches an engine, a driver or a Tk file.
- **Light is deliberately deferred (Mike's scope decision).** Only dark is offered, chosen at start-up and polished. `CALSUITE_THEME=light` and a saved 'light' are ignored by Qt (`theme.ENABLED_THEMES = ('dark',)`, `theme.DEFAULT_THEME`), and there is no Appearance menu (`theme.build_appearance_menu` returns None while one theme is enabled). Everything stays token-driven (palettes come from `ui_theme_core.LIGHT` / `DARK`, the QSS is generated from tokens, `theme.set_theme(name)` re-styles live and emits `theme.manager().changed`), so `set_theme('light')` works as plumbing, but the light look has not been reviewed or screenshot-checked and no light test exists. To enable it later: (1) add 'light' to `ENABLED_THEMES`; (2) run `python -m qtui.gallery --shots DIR --theme light` and fix what looks wrong (the usual suspects: disabled text, the primary / on_primary contrast, the badge pairs, row tints; the contrast test in `tests/test_qtui_theme.py` is dark-only and needs a light twin); (3) check every screen that paints its own colours reads `theme.tokens()` at paint time (the DataTable delegate and the icons do); (4) the Appearance menu appears by itself once two themes are enabled; (5) the Tk and Qt palettes are the same tokens, so a Tk 'light' saved choice then also applies to Qt.
- **Modules.** `theme.py`: tokens (`build_tokens`, derived colours: focus, disabled, placeholder, tints), `M` (the one place for sizes, spacing, radii, row / control heights, font pixel sizes; Qt device-independent px, so `ui_theme_core.px()` is not used), font choice with fallbacks (`font_family()` Segoe UI Variable / Segoe UI on Windows, Inter / Noto Sans / Ubuntu / DejaVu Sans elsewhere; `mono_family()` Cascadia Mono / Consolas, JetBrains / Noto Sans Mono / DejaVu Sans Mono), drawn icons (`icon`, `pixmap`; no image files shipped, the QSS check / chevron images are written once to a cache folder in the temp dir), `build_qss`, `build_palette`, `ThemeManager`. `widgets.py`: `Card`, `Badge` (good / warn / bad / info / neutral), `SegmentedFilter` (one Tab stop, arrow keys), `InfoButton` (popover), `PrimaryButton` / `SecondaryButton` / `DangerButton` / `GhostButton`, `SectionHeader`, `KeyValueStrip`, `Banner` (info / warn / error / good), `SearchField` (debounced `queryChanged`), `Toast`, `StatusLine` (dot colour from `ui_theme_core.status_kind`), `EmptyState`, `ModalDialog` (title, body, button row, Esc = cancel button, Enter = default button, centred on the parent, tab order = reading order), `MessageDialog` (info / warning / error / question, optional detail box), `ProgressDialog` (determinate or indeterminate; Esc and Cancel only raise `cancelled`, the owner calls `finish()`), helpers `label(text, role)`, `rule()`, `make_tab_bar()`, `set_prop()`. `table.py`: `DataTable` + `Column`. `app.py`: `create_app`, `run_app`, `post`, `call_on_ui_thread`, `after`, `set_icon_hook`, `set_log_hook`, exception hook. `gallery.py`: every widget on one page.
- **DataTable.** Column spec: key, title, width, align, `fmt`, `mono`, `kind` ('text' | 'badge' | 'combo' | 'checkbox' | 'progress'), `editable` (bool or row -> bool), `choices`, `badge_map` / `badge`, `tone`, `sort_key`, `natural` ('3-9' before '3-12'), `stretch`, `searchable`. Rows are any objects (dict, attribute object, tuple / list with an int key, or `getter`) and are never copied: `selected_rows()` returns the same objects (ROW_ROLE returns a `RowRef`, because Qt copies a dict that travels as a variant; use `table.row_of(index)`). Row colour: `row_tone(row)` -> 'pass' | 'fail' | 'warn' | 'info' | None (tint, plus red / amber text for fail / warn; `tone_style='tint' | 'text' | 'both'`). Sorting is done in the model with precomputed keys (empty values last both ways), filtering by a QSortFilterProxyModel (every word of the search text must occur in the searchable cell texts; plus an optional `set_predicate`), selection survives sort / filter / `set_rows` (by `row_key`), Ctrl+C and the context menu copy tab-separated text, `frozen=True` freezes column 0, uniform row heights (Fixed section mode). Signals: `selectionChangedRows`, `rowActivated`, `cellEdited`, `countsChanged`; `set_edit_hook(fn)` can reject an edit (writes are set, read back by the owner, never retried). Measured headless (offscreen, Python 3.12, this sandbox, 6 columns incl. badge / progress / checkbox): 50,000 rows load 110 ms, sort 175 to 315 ms, text filter 105 to 285 ms (the first filter builds the search text cache), predicate filter 350 ms, scroll or repaint of a screen full 25 ms, copy of 50,000 selected rows 0.5 s; 1,000 rows: every operation under 50 ms. Known slow spot: Select All on 50,000 rows takes about 2 s (Qt checks each cell's flags); everything else on a normal table is instant.
- **App and threads.** `create_app()` creates the one QApplication (Fusion style, `PassThrough` HiDPI rounding so 125 % / 150 % screens stay crisp, theme applied, UI font set, exception hook installed), `run_app(factory)` gives `factory` the main `QMainWindow` and runs the loop (same semantic as `ui_port.run_app`). `post(fn, *a)` is thread-safe and fire-and-forget (always queued, also from the UI thread). `call_on_ui_thread(fn, *a, timeout=None)` is `ui_port.call_from_worker` for Qt: the worker blocks until fn ran on the UI thread and gets its result; an exception in fn is RE-RAISED in the worker (the Tk port returns None; a Qt `UiPort` must wrap the call if an engine relies on None); on the UI thread it just calls fn; `timeout` raises TimeoutError and drops the call if it had not started; a closing application releases waiting workers with RuntimeError. Uncaught exceptions (main thread, worker threads, posted calls) always open a `MessageDialog` with the traceback (passwords scrubbed) and a Copy button; `set_log_hook(fn)` is where the host plugs `diagnostics.write_log` in.
- **How to add a screen.** (1) New module `qtui_<screen>.py` or a subpackage (not inside qtui/ if it needs an engine); build it from `widgets` / `table` only, set colours and sizes through `theme.M`, QSS properties (`role`, `surface`, `badge`, `banner`) and `theme.tokens()`; never `setStyleSheet` with a literal colour. (2) Window code reads the engine only through `ui_port` (`from ui_port import ui as port`) and gets its data from the view model the engine already exposes. (3) Worker threads touch the UI only via `app.post` / `app.call_on_ui_thread`. (4) Anything custom-painted reads `theme.tokens()` inside `paint` and repaints on `theme.manager().changed`. (5) Add the screen to the gallery if it introduces a widget, and a headless test that skips when PySide6 is missing (copy the header of `tests/test_qtui_table.py`).
- **Rules.** No Tk import anywhere in `qtui/` (tkinter, ui_theme, ui_shell, ui_port_tk, `*_view_tk`). `theme.py`, `widgets.py` and `table.py` import only PySide6, the standard library, `ui_theme_core` and `qtui`: no instrument, driver or engine module; `app.py` likewise. Engines are reached only through `ui_port`. `tests/test_qtui_rules.py` enforces this statically (it runs without PySide6). Instrument commands, limits, pass/fail and safety prompts are not touched by this package.
- **Tests (targeted; never the full suite).** `tests/test_qtui_theme.py` (token coverage against `ui_theme_core`, QSS resolved, dark contrast, dark-only choice, saved setting, live switch), `test_qtui_table.py` (sort, filter, selection, edit hook, copy, tone, frozen column, 50,000-row timing bounds), `test_qtui_app.py` (call_on_ui_thread success / exception / timeout, post order, exception dialog, run_app), `test_qtui_dialogs.py` (Esc / Enter, focus order, centring, progress, segmented filter, search field), `test_qtui_rules.py`. All Qt tests set `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` and skip when PySide6 is not importable. Run: `<venv python> -m unittest tests.test_qtui_theme tests.test_qtui_table tests.test_qtui_app tests.test_qtui_dialogs tests.test_qtui_rules`.
- **Not verified.** Never seen on Windows (fonts, DPI scaling, title bars: the screenshots are offscreen grabs without window chrome), never with a screen reader, only the dark theme was reviewed, the 'Inter' look in the screenshots depends on the font installed here (Segoe UI on the lab PCs will differ slightly in width: check no text clips on the first Windows run).

## Qt twins of Asset History, the report details window and the Review uncertainties window (QT-B, branch `qt-p3-history-review`, base `qt-migration` 0c42b5f; PySide6 6.12 offscreen, simulated benches and the stubbed Tk only; never seen on Windows, never with hardware)
Phase 3 screens, new files only; the Tk screens keep working and stay the default.
- **Files.** `qtui/screens/asset_history.py` (`AssetHistoryPanel(parent)`: twin of `labregistry_ui.HistoryPanel`; also `choose_action`, `open_drift_chart` + `DriftChart`), `qtui/screens/report_details.py` (`ask_details(parent, procedure)` with the contract of `report_dialog.ask_details`, `ask_details_plain`, `StandardsGrid`), `qtui/screens/report_review.py` (`ask(parent, procedure, app=None)`, `ReviewWindow(parent, model, procedure, demo, details, position)`; same names as the Tk module: `UNAVAILABLE`, `find_app`, `asset_of`, `SHORT`, `COVERED`), `report_review_flow.py` (toolkit-free, see below), tests `tests/test_qt_history.py`, `tests/test_qt_report_review.py`, `tests/_qt_test_support.py` (forces the tkinter stub also where a real tkinter exists, one QApplication per process, a real double-click).
- **Logic moved, behaviour unchanged.** The engine lookup and model building that sat in `report_review.ask()` (`find_app` by `app.root is parent`, `hint_kinds`, `asset_of`, `is_demo`, the run-queue loop over meters, the 'cannot be built -> plain popup' fallback, the closing `details['uncertainty_review'] = review_block(...)`) are now `report_review_flow.build_models()` / `finish()`; `report_review.py` (Tk) imports them and re-exports the old names, so `report_review.find_app`, `.asset_of`, `.COVERED`, `.UNAVAILABLE`, `.ask`, `.ReviewWindow` work as before. One detail: `hint_kinds` (WaveSurfer: reads the kind of `export_calibration_section` from the stack) now starts at its own caller and looks 12 frames up instead of starting at frame 3 and looking 6 up, so the extra frame of `build_models` does not change what it sees. The models are the same `report_review_model.py` / `report_review_adapters.py`; nothing was forked.
- **How the Qt entry is reached.** The engines call `port.report_details(...)` / `report_dialog.ask_details(self.root, ...)`. `report_dialog.ask_details` now begins with a 3-line switch: when `parent` is a Qt object (a PySide6 class in its MRO; PySide6 is never imported there) it returns `qtui.screens.report_details.ask_details(parent, procedure)`; anything else (a Tk root, None, a test double) follows the old path, so Tk is the default and its behaviour is untouched. A Qt `UiPort.report_details` can call `qtui.screens.report_details.ask_details` directly (same contract). The engine behind the window is found exactly as in Tk, by the object whose `.root` is `parent`, which needs the Qt build's engine to keep its main window in `self.root` (as every engine does).
- **Asset History.** Same constructor, public methods, texts and order of questions as Tk. Regenerate PDF: the save dialog (title 'Save regenerated report', initial name / folder as Tk, Qt's own overwrite question switched off because `overwrite()` asks 'Replace file?' with 'No' preselected and says when it is the original file), 'Rebuild from history rows' confirmation, the plain message afterwards; double-click on a report regenerates, right-click offers 'Regenerate PDF...' / 'Open report PDF', 'Open report PDF' hands the file to the system viewer (os.startfile / open / xdg-open as before). Dialogs are the module functions `_info`, `_ask_yes_no`, `_ask_ok_cancel`, `_error`, `_save_file`, `_open_file` (tests replace them). The Tk file had no delete or export in this tab, so none was added.
- **Review window.** Header with the status pills, the compact details strip (technician, temperature, humidity, default standard, TAR / TUR check boxes, 'Standards used...'), the filter (All / Needs attention / Changed by me), 'Set standard for selected rows' + Apply, the `DataTable` (combo cell per changeable row, badge status, `[type]` cell for a point the lab scope does not cover, locked rows as plain text), the hint banner, the note, Cancel / Reset changes / Generate PDF ('Next meter (n/m)' in a run queue). Overrides are stored exactly as before in `details['uncertainty_review']` (`review_block`), PDF layout untouched. Typed uncertainty: one click opens the editor with the Tk hint, Enter keeps it (a bad entry shows the reason and keeps the editor open with the text), an empty entry clears it, Esc or leaving the cell does not keep it.
- **DIFFERS from Tk (look and keyboard only).** (1) A check box column mirrors the row selection (the prototype has it); Ctrl / Shift-click still selects as in Tk. (2) The columns sort and resize (click a header); the Tk grid did not. (3) Enter on a row opens its drop-down (or the typed entry); not in Tk. (4) The details strip fields are always visible (as Tk) but 'Standards used...' is a dialog with the same three rows. (5) A pill row drops under the title below 1120 px width. (6) `[DEMO]` is appended to every window title in a demo run and the DEMO banner is shown, as in Tk. (7) Asset History has a splitter between its three tables, failed / overdue rows are tinted, the 'Last result' column is a badge.
- **Tests (targeted).** `python -m unittest tests.test_qt_history tests.test_qt_report_review` in a venv with PySide6 (reportlab, pypdf and pdftotext also needed); without PySide6 they skip. They cover: every cell of the Qt grid against a copy of the Tk `row_values`; standard change through the real cell editor recalculated by hand (`hand_budget`, as `test_reprint_review`); reported never below the scope minimum for every offered standard; bulk change; filters; default standard; Reset; 'No uncertainty required' locked rows; typed uncertainty (bad / good / clear / Esc / focus change) and its record (value, who, when); Generate and Cancel through `ask()`, the record equal to the toolkit-free model's replayed without a window; the PDF identical to the no-review PDF when nothing changed, the snapshot rows, Regenerate PDF identical; demo marking and no history row; all four phase-2 engines (MDO4000C, 33xxx, TDS2000C, WaveSurfer) build a window equal to the model; a run queue shows one review per meter in order; the plain details popup returns the Tk dict and remembers it; the `report_dialog` switch; every question and message of 'Regenerate PDF' (the Tk tests of `test_reprint_ui.py`, driven through the real widgets) and the PDF it writes.
- **Not verified.** Never seen on Windows or a real screen; the combo pop-up list is a separate native window that the offscreen grab does not show; fonts on the lab PCs (Segoe UI) are narrower or wider than the Inter used in the screenshots, check that nothing clips; no screen reader; dark theme only; no real instrument and no hardware.

## Qt Datasheet Builder (qtui/screens/, package QT-C1, branch `qt-p3-dsb`; checked headless with PySide6 offscreen only, never seen on Windows or a real screen; DARK ONLY)
The Qt twin of `datasheet_builder_ui.py` and `datasheet_builder_check_ui.py`. The toolkit-free `datasheet_builder_ui_model.py` (GridModel, DatasheetList, Storage, the check mixin) is REUSED unchanged: selection, keys, cell edits, `panel_state`, `calc_row` and saving all live there, so the Qt screens are thin views and no logic was moved. Tk files are untouched.
- **Entry.** `qtui/screens/datasheet_builder.py`: `DatasheetBuilderPanel(parent, storage, on_saved=None, status=None, matcher_factory=...)` with the same public methods as the Tk class (new, open_selected, duplicate, delete_or_restore, history, export, import_, refresh, say ...). Helper modules: `dsb_editor.py` (the grid window), `dsb_grid.py` (SheetGrid, a `DataTable` subclass with a cell delegate; model-owned selection and keys, so no Qt-driven navigation), `dsb_inspector.py` (the row panel), `dsb_pages.py` (Details, Functions, Ranges, Spec, Automation pages), `dsb_dialogs.py` (problems, preview, drivers, rule, versions, new-sheet), `dsb_check.py` (sheet check), `dsb_common.py` (Var / Bind give Qt widgets the Tk get()/set() API; table helpers; `ask_yes_no / ask_string / ask_save_path / ask_open_path` are module-level so tests patch them).
- **Preserved from the Tk builder.** Low / High limit cells are read-only and filled only by Calc (typing in one shows `LIMITS_HINT`, no edit); a bare number in a unit-ful cell shows with its unit (5 on a mV row displays +5 mV); the period picker is a label (1-year sheets, hidden for one period); stored typed limits keep the "typed" marker and the Clear typed limits button; the inspector auto-collapses below 1150 px (`inspector_should_be_open`) and a hand choice sticks; Run on real instruments asks first with No as default and lists unverified commands; a sheet with problems is never launched; Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+S / Ctrl+I act on the sheet even in a text box.
- **Native feel.** Real key handling on the grid (arrows, Tab / Shift+Tab over editable cells, Home / End, PageUp / PageDown, F2, Enter, Delete, a printable key starts an entry), mouse select / double-click, section headings span the columns, 450-row sheets stay fast (DataTable, rows never copied).
- **Same bytes as Tk.** `tests/dsb_scenario.py` is a toolkit-neutral script (header, function, typed cells, bare unit, calc, multi-point, handheld template, waivers, save, draft, run, versions ...); `tests/dsb_tk_reference.py` runs it against the Tk panel under `tests/_tk_stub.py` in a subprocess (it forces the stub even where a real tkinter exists); `tests/test_qt_dsb.py` runs it against the Qt panel and compares every message, every file under `user_specs` and every registry row (time stamps frozen by `dsb_scenario.frozen_time`).
- **Shared addition.** `qtui/theme.py` (append-only): QSS role `QPushButton[role="chip"]` (checkable pill for the tolerance-type toggles).
- **Tests.** `tests/test_qt_dsb.py` (52 tests; skipped without PySide6): editor, keyboard / mouse, inspector, run and layout, pages, list panel, speed, no-Tk-import and size rules, Tk-vs-Qt comparison.
- **Differs from Tk.** Tk Toplevels are non-modal Qt windows (`ModalDialog`); Yes / No questions use `MessageDialog`; the grid is a table view (sortable list tab, native scrolling); the Inspector's field-visibility rules are duplicated in Qt (not moved to the model).
- **Not verified.** Windows, real DPI scaling, a real screen, native file dialogs (patched in tests); the Tk reference run uses the tkinter stub, never a real Tk window.

## QT-F1: `QtUiPort`, the Qt shell and the toolkit selector (branch `qt-p3-uiport`, base `qt-migration` 0c42b5f; PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never seen on Windows, never run on hardware; DARK ONLY)
The Qt side of the UiPort seam and the window frame every Qt engine window sits in. Nothing here touches an engine, a driver, a Tk file or an instrument command.
- **`ui_port_qt.QtUiPort`** implements every method of `ui_port.UiPort` (the test `test_every_uiport_method_exists` compares it with `ScriptedUiPort`). Dialogs are the `qtui` `ModalDialog` / `MessageDialog` (`qtui/prompts.py`); labels are PLAIN text, so a text like `USB0::<mfgID>::INSTR` is shown literally. Safe answers: Esc / the window close button give No / Cancel / False / None exactly like the Tk dialogs; the preselected button is Yes (`default='no'` preselects No). `operator_prompt` shows heading, facts card, numbered steps, caution banner and the hook-up PNG (from `diagrams/`, drawn on white) beside the text; `layout='function_change'` draws the point strip + stepper from the `card` dict. `ask_number` reproduces `simpledialog`'s validation (`Illegal value` / `Too small` / `Too large`, the dialog stays open, `nan` accepted). A secret (`show='*'`) is masked in the entry and never recorded. File choosers are `QFileDialog` instances with the Tk options (`filetypes` -> name filters, `defaultextension`, `initialfile`, `initialdir`, `title`, `multiple`); native on Windows, the Qt dialog on the offscreen platform (`qtui.prompts.native_file_dialogs` overrides).
- **Threads, as the engines observe them.** `post` = `qtui.app.post` (thread safe, always queued). `after` returns a `QTimer` handle (`cancel_after` is new). `call_from_worker` reproduces the Tk behaviour: the worker blocks until the UI thread ran `fn` and gets its result; if `fn` raises the worker gets `None` and the traceback is reported on the UI thread by `qtui.app`'s exception hook (a dialog, never silent) without holding the worker up (the Tk port printed it on the UI thread; `qtui.app.call_on_ui_thread` itself re-raises in the worker, use it in new Qt code). Called on the UI thread it just calls `fn` (Tk would deadlock). An application that is closing releases the worker with `RuntimeError`.
- **Window plumbing.** `run_app` = `qtui.app.run_app` (win = the `QMainWindow`), `set_title`, `on_close` (an event filter: the close button calls the handler instead of closing; `close(win)` really closes), `bind_key` (Tk sequences like `<Control-s>` / `<F5>` / `<Escape>` become window shortcuts), `flush` (repaint, no user input), `update` (all events), `enable_hidpi` -> `qtui.app.configure_hidpi`, `var()` -> `qtui.vars.QtVar` (get / set / `trace_add('write', cb)` like Tk; `qtui.vars.bind(widget, var)` ties a QLineEdit / QComboBox / QCheckBox / QRadioButton / QLabel to one), `view(name)` -> `<name>_qt` (an `ImportError` naming the missing Qt twin if that view is not written yet), `surprise_handler` -> `surprise.tk_handler(win, schedule=...)` with a Qt timer (the Stop / Continue window is `surprise_view_qt.dialog`, which is NOT written by this package: until it exists a surprise is answered Stop, the safe way).
- **Toolkit selector** (append-only in `ui_port.py`): `select_toolkit(name=None)` installs `TkUiPort` or `QtUiPort` (`name` or env `CALSUITE_UI=qt|tk`, default `tk`; anything else is a `ValueError`) and returns it.
- **Handheld windows** (WP-H interface: `ask_reading` / `ask_range` / `ask_ir_problem`, `ask_readings`, `progress_window`) delegate like the Tk port to `view('handheld_dmm_view')` (= `handheld_dmm_view_qt`) and to `run_queue_ui_qt` (implemented by QT-H, see the QT-H section).
- **`qtui/shell.py`: `Shell`** is the twin of `ui_shell.Shell` with the same constructor and attributes, so a view ports by swapping the shell and keeping its layout code: `status_var` (a `QtVar`), `add_chip`, `set_modified` / `set_verified` / `set_devmode`, `build_identity`, `build_progress` (`ProgressPanel`: ring + `update_counts` / `set_rerun`), `notice`, `add_action(text, command, accent, danger, side, short, state)` (returns a `QPushButton` with Tk-style `configure(state=...)`), `set_sections` / `choose_section` / `build_section_picker`, `layout_hook`, and the containers `strip` (QHBoxLayout), `notice_holder`, `drawer_body` (QGridLayout, column 1 stretches), `drawer_footer`, `content` (QVBoxLayout). Below 1100 px the sidebar folds into the Section drop-down and the action buttons take their short texts (window minimum 900 x 640 fits). Additions: `add_menu(title)`, `log_line` / `show_log` (collapsible log pane), the connections drawer overlays the content (Esc closes it, focus returns to the button), sidebar rows are keyboard focusable. `results_table(app, parent, ...)` / `small_window(app, tree, filter_bar)` are the twins of the `ui_shell` functions; the table (`shell_helpers.ResultsTree`, a `DataTable`) answers the Treeview calls the views make (`insert`, `delete`, `get_children`, `selection`, `selection_set`, `see`, `exists`, `item`, `heading`, `column`, `configure(displaycolumns=)`, `bind('<<TreeviewSelect>>', fn)`) plus `set_all(rows, keep)`; failed rows are tinted, a PASS shows green text in the Result cell (100 tinted rows would shout), pending rows are muted. A Tk `side='bottom'` frame packed before the table is simply added after `results_table(...)` in the Qt view.
- **`qtui/shell_helpers.py`** is the list of every `ui_theme.*` the `*_view_tk.py` files call, with a Qt equivalent: `px`, `SCALE`, `C` (live tokens, same keys), `apply_theme`, `polish`, `fit_window`, `center_on`, `dialog`, `Pill`, `Notice`, `Banner`, `StatusBar`, `Summary`, `Tooltip`, `themed_menu` / `post_menu`, `make_table`, `is_dark`, `enable_hidpi`, and the toolkit-free ones re-exported from `ui_theme_core` (`si`, `si_one`, `si_row`, `fmt_num`, `report_filename`, `Settings`, `status_kind`); plus the ttk widgets as factories with Tk-style `configure` / `cget` (`button`, `entry`, `combobox`, `checkbutton`, `radiobutton`, `label_`, `frame`, `separator`) and Tk's geometry as `pack(parent, w, side=...)`, `grid(parent, w, row, column, sticky=...)`, `grid_weight`. NOT provided (not used by the engine windows of this package): `ui.attach_tree_menu`, `ui.attach_to_shell` / `attach_live` (procmods), `ui.ask_queue_setup` / `QueueProgress` (run queue): their Qt agents add them.
- **Shared-component fixes** (qtui, two lines each): `StatusLine` and `DataTable` connected a lambda to `theme.changed`, which fires on a destroyed widget the next time the theme is applied (an error dialog that blocked a test run); both now connect a bound slot, which Qt drops with the widget. New in `qtui/`: `prompts.py`, `vars.py`, `shell.py`, `shell_helpers.py`, `shell_demo.py`. Known wart left alone: `widgets.label()` creates AUTO-format labels, so rich-looking engine text in a plain `label()` would be read as HTML; `prompts.py` sets PlainText itself.
- **Demo / screenshots.** `python -m qtui.shell_demo [--shot out.png] [--size 1320x820] [--drawer]` opens the shell filled with the 34410A demo bench's own rows (`POINTS`, `DataModeMixin.shell_cells`), the screenshot is what was compared with the Tk window rendered under Xvfb.
- **Tests (targeted; never the full suite).** `tests/test_ui_port_qt.py`: every distinct recorded prompt of every `tests/golden_ui/*.json` fixture (695 distinct of the 1,739 recorded, 14 engines; the `ask_reading` ones belong to WP-H: messages, yes/no/ok/cancel/retry, text, number, value, operator prompts with their steps / facts / caution / buttons, file choosers with filters / default extension / initial file) played through `QtUiPort` by an auto-clicking operator that first checks title, text, button names and entry contents on screen, then gives the recorded answer; the returned value must equal the recorded one; the per-scenario sequence (kind, title, buttons) equals the Tk sequence; Esc / Enter / close answer the safe way; HTML-looking text is literal; secret entry masked; `ask_number` validation; worker-thread `call_from_worker` (result, dialog answered, cancel -> None, exception -> None + reported on the UI thread, UI-thread call, eight workers at once); `post` order; `after` / cancel; window plumbing; the selector. `tests/test_qt_shell.py`: the shell API, chips, identity binding, progress panel, action roles / states / compact texts, sections and the picker, the drawer (Esc, resize), status bar colours (also set from a worker thread), the Treeview-like table, `pack` / `grid` / `bind`, the demo window render at 1320 x 820 and 900 x 700. Both skip without PySide6. Run: `<venv python> -m unittest tests.test_ui_port_qt tests.test_qt_shell tests.test_qtui_rules` (as separate processes).
- **DIFFERS from Tk / unverified.** The dialogs look like the approved Qt design, not like Tk. `flush` repaints without handling input. `report_details` shows the plain popup (a Qt copy of `report_dialog.ask_details_plain`) until a `qtui.screens.report_review` module with `ask(parent, procedure)` exists. The numbered 'function change' stepper and the shell were only seen offscreen; Windows fonts and DPI, the native file dialog, title bars and real keyboard shortcuts were never seen; the `⚙` glyph on the Connections button depends on font fallback. The `*_view_qt.py` engine windows, `surprise_view_qt.py` and the handheld Qt windows are other packages.

## Qt package QT-D: Standards Specs tab and standards spec editor (branch `qt-p3-specs`; checked headless with PySide6 offscreen only, never on Windows or hardware; DARK ONLY)
- **Modules (new twins, no Tk file touched).** `qtui/screens/standards_specs.py` (`StandardsSpecsPanel(parent)`, alias `SpecManagerPanel`; same public methods as `specmanager_ui.SpecManagerPanel`; rail of standards, header card with confidence / divisor / period / range stats, bands + range table, chips, 'Edit spec...' / 'Export JSON...' / 'Check an EMU...', Import in the rail; below ~940 px the rail collapses to a pick bar) and `qtui/screens/spec_editor.py` (`EditorWindow`, `DetailsDialog`, `SaveDialog`, `HistoryWindow`, `RestoreDialog`, `open_editor(parent, model, on_saved, spec_dir, db_path, backup_dir)`). Models are REUSED unchanged: `spec_editor`, `spec_edit_io`, `spec_edit_log`, `specmanager`, `specmanager_view`, `uncertainty_budget`. The save / restore calls pass the same keyword arguments as the Tk window (proved by an AST check in the test).
- **Testability.** Message boxes, file dialogs and string prompts are module functions of `qtui/screens/spec_editor.py` (`ask_yes_no`, `show_info`, `show_warning`, `show_error`, `ask_save_path`, `ask_open_path`, `ask_string`, `text_dialog`); tests patch them. The panel calls them as `sed.<name>`.
- **Test.** `tests/test_qt_specs.py` (40 tests, skips without PySide6): the `checks_spec_editor` / `checks_standards_specs(_small)` scenarios of `tests/real_tk_checks.py` through real clicks and keys, plus `TkParity`: the same inputs through the Qt window and straight through the model give byte-identical spec JSON and backup files and identical edit-log rows and events (only `at` differs, not compared). Run: `<venv python> -m unittest tests.test_qt_specs`.
- **DIFFERS from Tk.** (1) The error message shows a drawn icon instead of the `x  ` text prefix. (2) Esc in the editor goes through the unsaved-changes prompt (Tk closed without asking). (3) The editor is a parentless top-level window. (4) Changed cells are an amber cell background plus outline, not overlay labels. (5) 'Close' buttons added to the EMU check and issues windows. (6) Banners and tooltips are Qt-native; editor grids are not sortable. (7) The design draft's 'Open source page...' button was NOT added. (8) The panel's minimum width hint is lowered so it can reach the pick-bar layout.
- **Not verified.** Windows (fonts, DPI), real screens, hardware. Offscreen screen is 800x600, which squeezes tall dialogs in screenshots only. No new shared `qtui/` components were needed. No sidecar lists `spec_editor*`, so no fingerprint changed.
## Qt twins of the shared helper windows (QT-F2) (branch `qt-p3-shared-views`; checked headless with PySide6 6.12 offscreen only; dark only)
`<stem>_view_qt.py` next to each `<stem>_view_tk.py` WP-S made, same public names (a test compares them), reached by `ui_port.view(name)` -> `<name>_qt`. No Tk import, no engine edited; toolkit-free `surprise.py`, `datamode.py`, `manual_override.py`, `procmods_engine.py` are called unchanged.
- **`surprise_view_qt.py`** `dialog(root, title, s)` (Stop only when `s.stop_only`, else Stop / Continue; Stop focused; closing / Esc = Stop; True only for Continue), `build(...)` (the widget, for tests), `schedule(ms, fn)` (a Qt single-shot timer) and `make_handler(win)` = `surprise.tk_handler(win, title, schedule=schedule)`. The worker-thread protocol is `surprise._TkAsker`, unchanged: the worker queues a request, a poller on the UI thread shows the dialog, nothing picks it up within `START_WAIT` (5.0 s: no event loop, or a handler made on a thread without one) = Stop. Proved with a real worker thread (dialog built on the main thread only, several asks in a row, Guard -> SurpriseStop) and with no loop (START_WAIT shortened in the test, shipped value asserted).
- **`datamode_view_qt.py`** `build_mode_bar` (radio buttons + hint), `build_mode_segment` (SegmentedFilter, hint = tooltip, amber Banner in `notice_holder`), `show_mode_notice`, `relabel_reading_column` (a `DataTable`'s 'reading' / 'found' / 'entered' column). The App gets the same attributes as in Tk: `mode_var` / `mode_hint` are `QtVar` (get / set / trace_add; setting one moves the control without calling `_mode_clicked`), `mode_buttons` are QAbstractButtons (an engine's busy/idle code calls `setEnabled`, not `configure(state=)`). `parent` is a QWidget (its layout) or a QLayout; the Tk `**pack` options are ignored.
- **`manual_override_view_qt.py`** `ask_reading_window(...)` (+ `build`): same texts and order as Tk, the failure / DEMO / entry-error lines are Banners, Enter = 'Use this reading  (Enter)', an invalid entry or a declined confirmation keeps the window open, closing / Esc = cancel.
- **`run_queue_view_qt.py`** `build_queue_drawer(self, d, row)` (`d` = the drawer's QGridLayout; rows row..row+2; `btn_queue`, `queue_label` as in Tk). `ask_queue_setup` / `queue_progress` resolve `run_queue_ui_qt.ask_queue_setup` / `.QueueProgress` at call time (QT-E ports run_queue_ui.py under that name).
- **`procmods_view_qt.py`** `attach_tree_menu` is native (Edit with the disabled reason cut at 90 characters, then `app.tree_menu_extras`, right-click selects the row first; a `DataTable` gets it through the new `context_provider` hook of `qtui/table.py`, any other QAbstractItemView through its context menu). The editor windows it opens are `procmods_point_ui_qt.open_point_window` and `procmods_ui_qt.attach_live`, resolved at call time: **those two Tk-only modules (about 1,650 lines) are not ported yet**, so Edit / 'Edit procedure' raise ImportError until they are (`pm_live_attach` already turns that into a warning).
- **`calibrator_fit_view_qt.py`** `attach_note(parent, offer, ui, widgets, row, columnspan, wrap)` (label under the picker, tooltip on the label and the pickers; `ui` is accepted and ignored).
- **Sidecars.** `surprise_view_qt.py` is listed beside `surprise_view_tk.py` in the 11 sidecars that list the Tk one (33220A, 33250A, 33210A, 34410A, 34411A, 34460A / 61A / 65A / 70A, 34970A, 34972A); `tools/ui_flips.py` shows exactly those 11 flipping to 'Changed since verification' (accepted by the owner; no instrument command changed).
- **Prompt fixtures.** The records of `ui_prompts_shared.json` are UiPort-level (messages, yes / no, operator prompts, reading windows of handheld_dmm); none is shown by these windows. `tests/test_qt_shared_views.py` replays the first four records of `overrides_datamode_note_reset_8808a` through `ManualOverrideMixin` with the Qt view installed and compares them with the fixture, and drives the entry window with QTest for the confirmation record (`Use this reading?`, Yes / No).
- **Tests.** `tests/test_qt_shared_views.py` (34 tests; skipped without PySide6). Not verified: Windows, real display, hardware.
## Fluke 8846A adjustment audit in lab_registry
- `Procedures/Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.py` no longer touches `calibration_procedures.sqlite3`. Its audit tables are `adjustment_runs_8846a` / `adjustment_events_8846a` (same columns as the old ones) in `lab_registry.sqlite3`, plus `adjustment_8846a_migration`. Suffixed because `adjustment_audit.py` (Keithley 2000 / 8808A) already owns `adjustment_runs` / `adjustment_events` there with different columns.
- First open copies any old `adjustment_runs` / `adjustment_events` rows once (old file opened `mode=ro`, ids kept, `INSERT OR IGNORE`, marker row); the old file is never altered or dropped. Instrument commands, limits and prompts unchanged.
- Sidecar fingerprint of the launcher changed: the 8846A Adjustment flips to 'Changed since verification' if it was marked verified. Test: `tests/test_8846a_adjustment_registry.py`.
- Open: a DEMO run still writes its rows to the registry (as before it wrote to the old file); the 2000 / 8808A use an in-memory store for demo.

## Qt twins of the procedure editor, per-point editor, run queue, readiness window and manual grid (QT-E) (branch `qt-p3-procmods`; checked headless with PySide6 offscreen only; dark only)
Window code in `qtui/screens/`; models and engines (`procmods_ui_model`, `procmods_point_model`, `procmods_engine`, `run_queue`, `run_queue_engine`, `tools/readiness_check.py`, `tools/readiness_run.py`) are called unchanged. No Tk import; worker threads reach the UI only through `qtui.app.post` / `call_on_ui_thread` (`screens/common.on_ui` / `post_ui`).
- **`procmods_editor.py`** (twin of `procmods_ui.py`): `shared_lock`, `UnlockDialog` / `ask_unlock` (500 ms lockout, entries wiped after submit, masked), the six tabs (`CommandsTab` ... `ChangesTab`), `ModifyWindow(parent, model, on_change=None)` (scope, search + filter, Apply / Cancel changes / Reset all / Import / Export, 'Unsaved changes' prompt on close), `ShellHook`, `LiveHook`, `attach_live`, `open_manager_changes`. `procmods_ui_qt.py` re-exports it (the module `procmods_view_qt.attach_live` resolves).
- **`procmods_point_editor.py`** (twin of `procmods_point_ui.py`): `PointWindow` (command table, selected-command editor, add-line, developer fields, scope, 'measure again', the pass/fail acknowledgement), `open_point_window(app, pid, lock=None, parent=None)`, `TreeMenu` / `attach_tree_menu` (kept for direct use; the engines use `procmods_view_qt`). `procmods_point_ui_qt.py` re-exports it.
- **`run_queue.py`** (twin of `run_queue_ui.py`; `run_queue_ui_qt.py` keeps the Tk module's names and signatures, so `handheld_dmm.ask_readings` callers need no change): `ReadingsDialog` / `ask_readings` (one box per meter, live PASS / FAIL and 'padded to', Enter moves on and records in the last box, No reading, Skip point, Stop run, time limit with the output switched off, demo Fill), `MeterDialog` / `ask_meter`, `QueueSetupDialog` / `ask_queue_setup`, `QueueProgress`. The ask_* functions run on the UI thread even when called from a worker.
- **`readiness.py`**: `start_readiness(parent, proc_id, proc_name, base, status=None, runner=None, on_done=None)` runs `tools/readiness_check.py <id>` in a worker thread (timeout 300 s, cwd = the Manager folder) and shows `ReadinessWindow` ('Readiness check - {name}'): a Findings tab (level filter, badge table, detail lines) over a Report text tab that holds the tool's text exactly. `parse_report` reads the `render()` text, so the headless tool is not touched.
- **`manual_grid.py`** (twin of `manual_grid.py`): `GridEntry(app, view)` on a `DataTable` whose `row_key` is the point id; same keys (Enter / F2 / typing opens, Enter / Tab / Down next, Up / Shift+Tab previous, Esc closes, F9 demo fill) and the same messages; a reading goes through `app.type_reading`.
- Tests: `tests/test_qt_procmods.py` (51), `test_qt_run_queue.py` (39), `test_qt_readiness.py` (15), `test_qt_manual_grid.py` (11); all skip without PySide6. No sidecar fingerprint lists any of the five Tk files, so there are no flips.
- Differs from Tk: Esc in the reading window = Stop run; lists are sortable and resize; the readiness window adds the findings list above the identical text; every dialog closes with Esc.
- Open: `qtui.table.DataTable` connects a theme lambda that raises when `create_app()` is called again after a table was closed (tests call it once per process). No Windows, no hardware.

## Qt Manager shell, QT-A (branch `qt-p3-manager`; PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never seen on Windows or a real screen, never with a real instrument; DARK ONLY)
The Calibration Procedure Manager in Qt: `qtui/screens/manager.py` (`ManagerWindow`) + launcher `Calibration_Procedure_Manager_qt.py` (the Tk launcher is unchanged). Behaviour, handlers, menu items, accelerators and status texts are the Tk Manager's.
- **Shared model, `manager_model.py` (toolkit-free).** The database registration (`init_db`, text moved unchanged: `Calibration_Procedure_Manager.init_db()` is now `manager_model.init_db(DB, BASE, records)`, the only change to the Tk Manager), the list of rows per view (find, category, verification status, 'MODIFIED (n)', user-built datasheets), the card, the row-menu rules, the launch command line (`launch_args`), the verified-on-hardware actions and their status texts, register / remove, the library folder text, backups / restore of a managed script. The Tk Manager's other methods were NOT rewritten (its tests drive them with fakes); parity of the two is proved by `tests/test_qt_manager.py` (rows of every view equal the Tk SQL + procscan rule) and by a one-off dump of the real Tk Manager under Xvfb against the Qt one (3 views: rows, every card text, the Find filter and its status text all identical; 37 / 0 / 3 rows).
- **Window.** Menu bar `File` (Exit, Ctrl+Q) / `Library` (Alt+L; Import Mudcats datasheet (.mdd)..., Import procedure package..., Register procedure..., info line 'Library folder: ...', Library folder..., Remove from library; Remove enabled only with a selection; the info line refreshes when the menu opens) / `Help` (About Calibration Suite F1, Open my data folder, Import data from another copy..., Save diagnostic log for support, Appearance with its one choice Dark). Header, DB chip, main tabs Procedures / Asset History / Standards Specs / Datasheet Builder (Ctrl+F = Find), Procedures sub-tabs Automated Calibrations / Manual Calibrations / Adjustments (the adjustment and manual notes), Find + category + Clear, `DataTable` (Procedure, Manufacturer, Model, Category, Status, Verified on hardware; sortable; Enter / double-click = Run), Selected-procedure card (title, sub line, 'Ready to run' / 'Script file not found', MODIFIED chip, description, file / version; PINNED below the scrolling part: verification pill + info button, Run, Try in demo mode, Fast demo (no windows), hint, Mark verified... / Unlock...), status bar (`StatusLine`, 'Find instruments...', version). The right-click menu (Shift+F10 / Menu key too) is `procedure_menu` incl. 'Readiness check (no hardware)...' (tools/readiness_check.py in a subprocess, output in a window).
- **Launching (same path as Tk).** `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, script, '--demo', '--fast-demo', '--asset X', ...], cwd=script folder)` through `ManagerWindow.launcher(args, cwd=...)` (tests give a fake). The child inherits `CALSUITE_UI`.
- **`CALSUITE_UI` (qt | tk, default tk until the cut-over).** `ui_port.py` got ONE append-only block: `toolkit_name()`, `select_toolkit(name=None)` and `DEFAULT_TOOLKIT = 'tk'` (the one line to change at the cut-over); `get_ui()`'s default now calls `_default_port()` (one line changed: it asks `toolkit_name()`, so an engine started on its own gets the same choice). 'qt' imports `ui_port_qt` and uses its `make_port()` or `QtUiPort()`; while that module does not exist (QT-F1 owns it) it falls back to the Tk port with a note on stderr. The Qt launcher calls `select_toolkit('qt')` for the Manager's own process. `tools/ui_flips.py` against origin/qt-migration: no procedure flips (ui_port.py and the Manager are in no fingerprint).
- **Hosted panels.** `qtui/screens/__init__.py` `PANELS` (append-only registry: 'asset_history', 'standards_specs', 'datasheet_builder' -> 'module:Class', imported lazily). A missing module shows an EmptyState 'X is not built yet', a panel that raises while it is built shows 'could not be built' with the error; the shell always works. Constructor arguments are filtered by signature: `on_saved=` only to the Datasheet Builder (it refreshes the Procedures list), `status=` is the Manager's status object (callable AND `.set` / `.get` like the Tk StringVar). `panel.refresh()` is called when its tab is shown. `win.history_panel / specs_panel / dsb_panel` keep the Tk attribute names.
- **Dialogs (Qt twins, same wording and rules).** `manager_dialogs.py`: messages / yes-no / text (replace messagebox / simpledialog), `ask_mark` / `ask_unlock` (verified_ui; a wrong entry is explained inside, the dialog stays open), package windows (packages_ui: Install stays disabled until the box is ticked), Register Calibration Procedure, Restore previous version, readiness output, the Mudcats .MDD import preview (mdd_import_ui; nothing is created until 'Create manual procedure'). `find_instruments.py` (instrument_scan_ui: scan in a worker, polled by a timer; Cancel, Remember / Forget, serial check, 'Show what was sent', chooser mode `ask_resource`). `help_menu.py` (branding.py: title pattern, About, data folder, import data, diagnostic log; `install_hooks()` plugs `diagnostics.write_log` and the app icon into `qtui.app`). File pickers go through `pick_open_file` / `pick_folder` so tests replace them.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_qt_manager.py` (60 tests, skipped without PySide6; run each class as its own process): table equals the Tk rows in each view, find / category / views / 'matches in another tab', card follows the selection and its pinned buttons, menus and accelerators, Run / demo / fast demo / user datasheet through a fake launcher (exact argv and cwd), verified flow + info popup, row menu, register / remove / package / MDD / Find instruments, hosted-panel registry (missing module, fake panel, failing panel), `select_toolkit`. Edited existing test: `test_manager_tabs` reads the CREATE TABLE text from `manager_model.py` (it moved there). Tk side checked after the change: test_procscan, test_verified_ui, test_procedure_menu, test_manager_tabs, test_packages_ui, test_dsb_runtime, test_qtui_rules, test_ui_port_* OK; the real-Tk Manager window checks construct the Manager and pass until a step that needs `reportlab` (not installed in that Python).
- **DIFFERS / not verified.** (1) The Tk Manager coloured the whole row (missing = red, modified = amber, verified = green); Qt colours the Status and Verified cells (and the name for missing / modified). (2) Appearance shows its single choice 'Dark (current)' (disabled) because the brief asks for the item; `theme.build_appearance_menu` adds nothing while only dark is enabled. (3) The tabs hold the panels directly (no outer scroll page as in Tk): a panel scrolls itself. (4) The card width follows the window (340 to 380 px). (5) The Tk status line after start-up shows the Procedures-tab text (its notebook event fires after the first refresh); the Qt window reproduces that with a 0 ms timer. (6) Not verified: any look on Windows, the file pickers (patched in tests), `Show file location` (xdg-open / explorer), launching a real child process in Qt (tests use a fake launcher; the argv equals the Tk one), the Qt engine windows (do not exist yet), the three hosted panels (other packages).
QT-F1 addendum (after merging origin/qt-migration with WP-H and QT-F2): `ask_reading`, `ask_readings`, `ask_range`, `ask_ir_problem` and `progress_window` of `QtUiPort` route to
`handheld_dmm_view_qt` / `run_queue_ui_qt` (now present: QT-H). `tests/test_ui_port_qt.py` skips only `ask_typed` and `prerun_list`, which are proved in tests/test_qt_dsb_runtime.py.

## QT-H: handheld DMM windows and the user-datasheet runtime on Qt (branch qt-p4-handheld)

- **Built.** `handheld_dmm_view_qt.py` (main window, table helpers, reading, IR-problem and dial windows, queue chip, iFlex bar hook), `iflex_option_view_qt.py`; the five `QtUiPort` methods (`ask_reading`, `ask_readings`, `ask_range`, `ask_ir_problem`, `progress_window`) now delegate directly, the NotImplementedError fallbacks are gone.
- **dsb runtime port.** `dsb_runtime.py`, `dsb_override.py`, `dsb_prerun.py` no longer import tkinter: their windows moved unchanged in behaviour to `dsb_runtime_view_tk.py`, `dsb_override_view_tk.py`, `dsb_prerun_view_tk.py` and have Qt twins `*_view_qt.py` (reached by `port.view(...)`). `dsb_prerun.list_text(info)` is shared; `main()` uses `port.run_app`. New fixtures `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_dsb.json` (12 scenarios) and `ui_prompts_dsb_main.json` (2) were recorded from the UNPORTED runtime, then reproduced at Tk level, Port level and through real Qt dialogs. The planner's hash-ordered 'assigned standard' is normalised to CAL in the scenario.
- **Tests (each class/module its own process).** `tests/test_qt_views_handheld.py` (21: 15 families + 3 handheld scenarios through QtUiPort and real Qt dialogs, engine-level 117 / 289 / 376+iFlex / MM325 / two-meter queue compared with a Tk reference run in a subprocess on the stubbed tkinter, busy/idle widget state vs Tk `sync_buttons`), `tests/test_qt_dsb_runtime.py` (11), `tests/test_ui_port_qt.py` (28). Helpers: `tests/qt_handheld_harness.py` (QtHarness / ProofPort), `tests/handheld_engine_cases.py`. `test_ui_port_adjust` needs tkinter and fails in a venv without it (pre-existing).
- **Found and fixed.** The reading dialog's countdown clock was never started in the first Qt build (caught by a test). Offscreen `activeWindow()` can be a stale closed dialog: harness deletes leftovers. Wrapped QLabels in a QGridLayout must not have a max width (height-for-width clips them). `qtui/app.py`: `run_app` returns 0 without an event loop when the factory already closed the window.
- **Sidecars.** The Qt view files are in the same fingerprint code lists as their Tk twins for the 19 handheld procedures (tools/ui_flips.py; flips accepted, 117 seeded-verified).
- **DIFFERS.** Notes wrap at full column width; at the 1000x640 minimum with demo notice + iFlex bar + queue + the long 376 note the content needs about 780 px and the info block (opaque) overlays the foot of the table; no 'Help' menubar in the Qt shell; dialogs without a parent attach to the active window; `report_details` with scripted details is not driven; file choosers are cancel-only in the proof.
- **Unverified.** No Windows, no hardware or real instruments, dark only, native file dialogs; no SCPI or limits were touched. Possible latent `setFont` vs `* {font-size}` QSS issue in QT-E's `qtui/screens/run_queue.py` (not changed).


## QT-G1a: Qt twins of the bench verification windows (branch qt-p4-bench-views)

Built `truevolt_view_qt.py` (34460A/61A/65A/70A), `dacq_view_qt.py`, `k2000_view_qt.py`, `f8808a_view_qt.py`, `a34401a_view_qt.py`, `f8846a_view_qt.py`, `agilent34410a_view_qt.py`, each next to its Tk twin and resolved by `get_ui().view(name)` under QtUiPort. No engine .py edits (no additive hooks were needed). Shared new module: `qtui/bench_view.py` (Form, drawer footer, action bar, results/detail builder, notice banner, table helpers, `fit_wrapped`, `_AfterRoot` shim for labregistry). One small change to `qtui/shell.py`: the progress-ring text shrinks to fit the ring.

Proof: `tests/test_qt_views_bench.py` (widget-state dump vs a dump of the real Tk windows under Xvfb, `tests/golden_ui/qt_views_bench_widgets_tk.json`; result rows and detail text vs Tk; interaction; render; sidecars) and `tests/test_qt_views_bench_prompts.py` (real engines on the simulated bench through the Qt view reproduce every Tk golden prompt sequence). Run each module separately: `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen <venv python> -m unittest tests.<module>`. Screenshots: `python tests/qt_bench_shots.py OUT_DIR [keys]` (or `QT_BENCH_SHOTS=dir`).

DIFFERS from Tk: Qt Help menu and shell Log button; window title built via `help_menu.dress` (same pattern); tree selection signal is deferred one event-loop turn; wrapped notes use `_FitHeight` so text is never clipped.

Unverified: no Windows, no hardware, offscreen rendering only; openpyxl is absent here so the 8846A Excel export is faked in tests; the prompt tests use a hybrid port (Qt window plus the harness prompt methods), not the real modal dialogs, except the surprise dialog which is exercised for real.

Sidecars: the 12 verification procedures list each `*_qt.py` right after its Tk view plus `qtui/bench_view.py` (flips shown with `tools/ui_flips.py`, exactly these 12).
## Qt twins of the function-generator and scope windows (QT-G2a) (branch `qt-p4-genscope-views`; PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never seen on Windows or a real screen, never with a real instrument; DARK ONLY)
`fgen_view_qt.py` (33220A / 33250A / 33210A), `mdo4000c_view_qt.py`, `tds2000c_view_qt.py`: the Qt twins of the three `*_view_tk.py` windows, resolved by `ui_port.ui.view('<name>_view')` (`<name>_qt`). Same functions as the Tk views (`setup_window`, `finish_window`, `build_ui`, `sync_buttons`, `table_*`, `set_choices`), same attributes on the engine's App (`tree`, `btn_run`, `entries` / `addr_boxes`, `cfg_widgets`, `period_combo` ...), same labels / button names / order / defaults / limits (the engines are untouched; MDO and TDS keep their request queue and 50 ms `_pump`, which only call the port). Built on `qtui.shell` (header, section sidebar, Connections drawer, action bar, status bar, compact layout below 1100 px). The shared parts of the three views are in **`genscope_support_view_qt.py`** (its name ends in `_view_qt.py` so the fingerprint test `test_procscan` accepts it next to the views): drawer rows, action bar, filter bar, detail text, busy state, the `table_*` calls, `widen_columns`, and `AfterRoot`.
- **`AfterRoot`**: `labregistry.attach(root, ...)` debounces the Instrument ID with `root.after` / `root.after_cancel` (Tk). A QMainWindow has neither, and `attach` swallows every error, so on Qt the serial / history line would silently never appear. The Qt views hand it `AfterRoot(root)` (timers of the port); proved with a typed Instrument ID that fills the serial and the status line.
- **Worker hand-off.** fgen's typed reading is `port.call_from_worker(root, ask)`: the QtUiPort already blocks the worker and runs the real `ask_number` box on the UI thread. `HandOff` (tests/test_qt_views_genscope.py) proves it with REAL Qt dialogs on the real 33220A window, two demo points: the box is created on the UI thread, the worker is alive and blocked while it is open, the point is NOT recorded until OK and then holds exactly the typed value; Cancel gives the worker `None` ('Reading not entered; run stopped.', point not recorded); an exception inside the box gives `None`, is reported on the UI thread by `qtui.app`'s hook and ends the run with the same error (the Tk behaviour of `tests/real_tk_wpg_fgen.py`). `SurpriseHandOff`: stop-on-surprise from a worker thread through `port.surprise_handler(window)` shows the real Stop / Continue window (`surprise_view_qt`) and waits: Continue True, Stop and the window's X False; like Tk, a demo window makes no handler.
- **Same results as Tk.** (1) `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_{fgen,mdo4000c,tds2000c}.json` (recorded on the unported Tk engines): the scenario functions of `tests/ui_prompt_capture_wpg.py` run UNCHANGED on `tests/qt_genscope_harness.QtHarness` (real engine on the simulated bench, real Qt window, real timers / threads, only the dialogs scripted): every scenario's prompt sequence equals the fixture (`PromptsFgen`, `PromptsMdo` ~70 s, `PromptsTds`). (2) Windows really shown: `RealDialogsFgen / Mdo / Tds` run a whole virtual run through the REAL `QtUiPort` (hook-up prompts, message boxes, typed readings) with a timer clicking the affirmative button; the (title, buttons) sequence of the windows equals the fixture's. (3) Rows / status / detail: `tests/record_tk_genscope.py` (real Tk 9 under Xvfb, uv Python 3.13: `xvfb-run -a <py> tests/record_tk_genscope.py`) ran each scenario in the REAL Tk window and recorded `tests/qt_ref/genscope_rows.json` (title, all table rows with values and tags before and after the run, status and detail text, sections, pass / fail counts, the three status filters, a chosen section, the detail text of selected rows); `Rows*` run the same driver (`tests/genscope_rows.py`) behind the Qt window and compare field by field: 33220A, 33250A with failures, 33210A, MDO4000C, MDO4000C with failures, MDO4000C with the SA6 option (the 96270A RF level / flatness rows, 611 rows), TDS2024C, TDS2012C with failures. The simulated bench measures perfectly, so 'with failures' moves every fifth numeric reading outside its limits as it is recorded (the same wrapper in both toolkits; it changes no engine file).
- **Window tests** (`WindowFgen / Mdo / Tds`, shared mixin): every function of the Tk view exists; every constant text of the Tk view (labels, buttons, check boxes, `add_action`) and every constant `values=` list is on screen; busy / idle state of every button and configuration box; action-bar order and roles; short texts and the section drop-down at 900 px; the drawer (opens, address boxes editable, Esc closes); `set_choices` keeps the typed address; row selection fills the detail text; the Instrument ID box is bound; As Left toggle; the status-filter lists (`Skipped` for MDO / TDS); Ctrl-S; closing the window through the engine's `on_close`; the period / test-set boxes drive the rows (fgen); option check boxes drive `read_cfg` (MDO); no Tk import; sidecars list the Qt files next to the Tk twin.
- **Fingerprints.** The five sidecars (33220A, 33250A, 33210A, MDO4000C, TDS2000C) list `<view>_view_qt.py` and `genscope_support_view_qt.py` right after the Tk view. `python tools/ui_flips.py <base checkout> .`: exactly those five procedures show 'Changed since verification'. **Tektronix TDS2000C is `seed_verified: true` (verified on hardware): it flips and needs the owner's sign-off** (no instrument command, limit, pass / fail rule or prompt text changed).
- **Two small fixes in shared QT-F1 files** (found by these windows): (1) `ui_port_qt.QtUiPort.close(win)` called from the engine's `on_close` handler (the window's X) did nothing: Qt ignores a `close()` inside the window's own close event, so no engine window could be closed with its X button. `close` now closes again once that event is over (`QTimer.singleShot(0, win.close)`); `tests/test_ui_port_qt` unchanged and green. (2) `qtui.shell._Ring`: three-digit counts ('496/496', '611/611') overran the progress ring; the font shrinks until the text fits.
- **DIFFERS from Tk.** (1) The MDO / TDS model drop-down shows 'MDO4104C' / 'TDS2024C'; Tk showed the bare '4104C' the engine's `on_config` writes into the variable (a Tk combobox shows whatever text its variable has; a Qt drop-down shows an item), so the Qt box FOLLOWS the variable to the prefixed item (`follow_prefixed`). (2) Tk text columns are wider in characters; the Qt Low / High / Error columns are widened (`widen_columns`) so '9.99998 MHz' and '+246.373 mdB' are not cut. (3) A Qt check box answers a click on its box or text only (not on the empty row); a Qt table is sortable and resizable. (4) The window title bar, fonts, drop-down popups and dialogs are the Qt design system's. (5) Tk's `Combobox` values for `set_choices` keep the typed address in both; the TDS engine assigns its own `values` (as in Tk). No Alt accelerators exist in the Tk views, so none were lost.

Offscreen screenshots only. Sidebar names are elided at 224 px (shared shell).

Helpers (not tests): `tests/qt_genscope_harness.py`, `tests/genscope_rows.py`, `tests/record_tk_genscope.py`, fixture `tests/qt_ref/genscope_rows.json` (rewrite it only for an INTENDED change of what these windows show). ## Qt twins of the three guided Adjustment windows (QT-G1b) (branch `qt-p4-adjust-views`; checked headless with PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never on Windows or hardware; DARK ONLY)
`keithley2000_adjust_view_qt.py`, `fluke8808a_adjust_view_qt.py`, `fluke8846a_adjust_view_qt.py` next to their `_tk` twins: the same public functions with the same signatures (a test compares them), reached through `ui_port.ui.view('<name>_adjust_view')` -> `<name>_adjust_view_qt` under `QtUiPort`. The three launchers (`Procedures/Keithley_2000_Adjustment.py`, `Fluke_8808A_Adjustment.py`, `Fluke_8846A_Adjustment.py`) are NOT edited: every guard (typed SAVE / COMMIT phrase, committed flag, As Left verification warning, reset confirmation, relock, 'Lock first' / 'Relock first' on close, masked calibration code / password) lives in them and runs unchanged behind the Qt window. The views only lay out widgets and map the launcher's booleans onto buttons and pills. Shared layout code is `qtui/adjust_window.py` (page frame in a scroll area, `FieldGrid`, step / audit tables, notice, button helper); no Tk, no engine.
- **Same behaviour.** Window title (`... [DEMO]`), banner + pills (DEMO / connected / record / LOCKED / UNLOCKED, same texts and kinds), the red 'This changes the instrument calibration' notice, 'Who and what' and 'Connect' cards with every field of the Tk window (scope pickers lock once a record is active; 8808A `*TER` tick box off by default and marked UNVERIFIED; 8846A port picker is editable and keeps a typed name), the NEXT line, step table in procedure order (the header does not sort: order is a safety property), button bar in the Tk order and text, 'Show audit log' (8846A starts hidden, the other two shown), audit table with the newest row first and ok / failed outcomes coloured, 8846A 'Selected step' pane. Row tags map to tones: done = green, next = blue, failed = red, 8846A eligible = blue.
- **Proof.** `tests/test_qt_views_adjust.py` (24 tests, skipped without PySide6; run on the PySide6 venv python, about 65 s): (1) the real launchers on a real `QMainWindow` with the Qt view, driven through the SAME sequences as the Tk scenarios (`tests/ui_prompt_capture.py` `adjustments` and `adj8846a`, over `C._FakeMeter8846A`), give prompt sequences EQUAL to `tests/golden_ui/ui_prompts_adjustments.json` and `ui_prompts_adj8846a.json`; (2) for every one of the 2^n inputs `sync_buttons` / `sync_pills` of the Qt view equal the Tk view run over fake widgets (button enabled <-> `state='normal'`, pill text and kind); (3) after every `_sync` each button is enabled exactly when the launcher said so, through a whole demo adjustment of the Keithley and the 8808A and the 8846A on its fake meter (connect, start, unlock, steps, commit, As Left reminder, relock); (4) the calibration code / password never appears in any label, table cell or entry of the window, nor in the 8846A registry rows; with the REAL `QtUiPort` the unlock and relock dialogs are masked (`EchoMode.Password`), Esc on the password leaves the calibration locked, refusing the authority question never asks for a password, declining the reset notice starts no record; (5) closing the window while unlocked asks first ('Lock first' / 'Relock first') and the window stays open, a malformed relock password is 'Relock not confirmed' and the calibration stays UNLOCKED.
- **Shared fix** (`qtui/prompts.py`, 6 lines): a secret entry (`show='*'`) is cleared and its dialog deleted after use. The dialog is a child of the launcher's window and was never deleted, so the typed password stayed in a hidden line edit of that window (found by the 'no password anywhere in the window' test).
- **Sidecars.** The three `.procedure.json` list the Qt view right after the Tk view in `fingerprint.code` and `own_files`; `tools/ui_flips.py` flips exactly Keithley 2000, Fluke 8808A and Fluke 8846A Guided Adjustment (accepted by the owner).
- **DIFFERS from Tk (look only).** The 'Connect' card flows its label / control pairs into as many columns as fit (Tk: one fixed grid that clips below ~1100 px); the body sits in a scroll area, so a small window scrolls instead of squeezing; the default window is the Tk size, the audit table shows 3 rows (Tk 6) and the step table takes the free height; the step to run next is scrolled into view (Tk left the list where it was); empty audit table says 'No audit entries yet'. Demo runs of the 8846A launcher still write to the real `lab_registry.sqlite3` (unchanged, flagged for Mike); the Keithley demo prompt still prints the simulated code (`SIM_CODE`) in its text, as in Tk.
- **Screenshots** (dark, offscreen vs real Tk 9 under Xvfb, idle / in step / error, each window) were viewed and compared; fixed: clipped combo texts and labels, overlapping rows under vertical squeeze (scroll area), a scroll bar at the default size, truncated time / step columns, audit table taller than the step list.
- **Not verified.** Windows fonts and DPI, a real display, real instruments (the simulated Keithley / 8808A benches and a fake 8846A serial meter only), the real 8846A serial path.

## Qt polish pass: datasheet builder and engine windows (branch `qt-polish-engines`, base `qt-migration` fd76ce1; PySide6 6.12 offscreen only, never seen on Windows or a real screen; dark stays the default, light enabled)
Light-mode and spacing review of the Datasheet Builder and every engine window, each rendered in both themes at 1920 x 1080 and at its 1000 x 640 / 1100 x 700 minimum (idle, drawer open, running, finished with failures, demo banner), looked at, fixed and re-rendered. Behaviour, texts, shortcuts, instrument commands, limits and pass / fail are unchanged.
- **Detail text over the table (the known 376 overlap, found in every results window).** `qtui/bench_view.py: DetailBox` holds the detail line and the source note in a scroll area that asks for the height of its text but can give up to three lines, so a long note scrolls instead of painting over the last table rows at 1000 x 640. Used by the seven bench windows (`build_results`), handheld / iFlex (`handheld_dmm_view_qt`, replaces the opaque `hhInfo` overlay), fgen / mdo4000c / tds2000c (`genscope_support_view_qt.detail_panel`) and WaveSurfer. `fit_wrapped` / `_FitHeight` no longer raise when the label is gone.
- **Column distribution.** `bench_view.ShareColumns` / `share_columns(tree, shell, compact=None, **base)`: the small window's merged Limits column gets 220 px (it was cut to "10.001..."), a table narrower than 900 px gets the merged Limits columns even when the window is not yet 'small' (WaveSurfer at its 1148 px layout minimum), and a wide window shares the spare width between the number columns instead of one huge Description column (the Manager-list idea). Used by bench, handheld (Calibrator output stays 150 px), generator / scope (`genscope_support_view_qt.share_table`) and WaveSurfer.
- **Datasheet Builder.** `dsb_grid._ShareSheet`: the sheet no longer gives every spare pixel to FUNCTION TESTED (the number columns share the width, the function column keeps the largest part); narrower than the columns' own widths the function column gives way first, as the stretch column did. `dsb_common.ShareWidth` (installed by `C.table` for any table with a stretching column) does the same for the list, the check / problems / versions windows and the page tables. Adding a function still puts the cursor on the empty row under its heading (untouched; the 52 builder tests include it).
- **Adjustment windows.** `qtui/adjust_window.py`: `button_bar` returns the same layout but wraps its second group (audit-log tick box and the closing buttons) onto a second row in a narrow window (`_ReflowBar`); `FlowRow` wraps a button row; `FieldGrid.minimumSizeHint` is one column wide. The 8846A page no longer needs a horizontal scroll bar at 1100 px (its notice used to be cut off).
- **Light mode review.** Reading dialogs, IR / range dialogs (the dial picture is a drawn illustration with its own dark colours, kept), surprise window, manual-override and typed-reading windows, pre-run list, the bench / handheld / generator / scope / WaveSurfer windows, the three adjustment windows, the builder editor, inspector, pages, check / preview / versions windows: PASS / FAIL colouring, banners, badges, progress ring, drawer overlay, selection, zebra rows and focus ring read correctly; no hard-coded dark colour was found in the owned files (every colour comes from `theme.tokens()`; the only literals are the dial illustration's).
- **Live switching.** `tests/qt_polish_live_switch.py` (subprocess of the test): a bench window and the builder editor built in dark repaint light and back with `theme.set_theme` and no restart.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_qt_polish_engines.py` (12: colour pairs in both themes, every bench window / handheld 376 / three generator-scope windows / WaveSurfer / adjustment windows / small dialogs / builder editor and list built in LIGHT at the minimum and wide with the no-overlap, column-width and no-horizontal-scroll checks, the button-bar wrap, the live-switch subprocess). Existing modules unchanged and green: test_qt_dsb (52), test_qt_dsb_runtime (11), test_qt_views_bench, test_qt_views_bench_prompts (7), test_qt_views_handheld (21), test_qt_views_genscope (every class, run separately), test_qt_view_wavesurfer, test_qt_views_adjust (24), test_qt_shared_views (34), procscan, golden_commands, ui_port_clean, fingerprint_isolation, qtui_rules; `git diff origin/qt-migration -- tests/golden` is empty. The sidecar fingerprints already list the edited `*_view_qt.py` files; `qtui/bench_view.py` and `qtui/adjust_window.py` are not fingerprinted.
- **Shared-component issues (not edited here, owners: qtui/theme.py, widgets.py).** (1) `theme.build_appearance_menu` connects `manager().changed` to a lambda that raises 'QAction already deleted' once the window that owns the menu is closed; with light enabled every `apply_theme` of a later window hits it and the error dialog of `qtui.app` blocks tests (and a Manager that opens and closes windows). Fix: connect a function that catches RuntimeError (`def _sync(n): try: acts[n].setChecked(True) ... except RuntimeError: pass`), or disconnect on `sub.destroyed`. (2) Light tokens: `disabled_fg` on `disabled_bg` 2.7, `placeholder` on field 3.1, `faint` on bg 2.4, `focus_ring` on bg 2.4, `on_primary` on `primary` 4.0 and on `danger` 3.5 (bold button text, fine for large text); dark `bad` on `select` 4.16 and `on_primary` on `primary` 2.9. (3) A disabled danger button (Stop while idle) keeps a pink fill in light and a dark red in dark and reads as live; a neutral disabled style would be clearer. (4) `Banner` with four pills (the Keithley 2000 adjustment) cannot wrap and sets the window's minimum width to about 1270 px. (5) The shell title wraps to two lines at 1000 px for long model names (34410A, WaveSurfer) which takes about 30 px of the table's height.
- **Not verified.** Windows fonts and DPI, a real display (offscreen grabs only), the combo pop-up lists (separate native windows), real instruments. The generator / scope windows grow to about 1076 px wide at their layout minimum (the 1000 px request is not reached by their header row).

Screens reviewed in dark AND light (Manager 1920 / 1100 / 2560, Find instruments, Readiness window, run-queue set-up / meter / readings dialogs, engine shell demo 1920 / 1100 / 2560, message / yes-no / text / secret / number / mark-verified / unlock / register / restore / text-window / operator prompt with and without hook-up PNG, gallery).

Used for the Toast dot, the verified-dialog error line, the message-dialog icon and every run-queue box (`_box_style`); these used to keep the colours they were built with.

## Qt polish pass: data screens (branch `qt-polish-data`, base `qt-migration` fd76ce1; light + spacing; offscreen PySide6 only, never seen on Windows)
Screens: asset_history, standards_specs, spec_editor, report_review, report_details, procmods_editor, procmods_point_editor, manual_grid. Behaviour, texts, shortcuts, limits and pass / fail are unchanged.
- **`qtui/screens/_share.py` `ShareWidth(table, base_widths, weights)`**: the idea of manager.py's `_ShareWidth` for my screens: the width beyond the base widths is shared between the columns, so a wide window is not one huge text column with the others pushed to the far right. Used by Asset History (three tables), the spec editor grids, the procedure editor Changes tab and the point editor command table. Standards Specs has its own `_fit_columns`; its weights now give the Range column (group titles) and Value span more room and Frequency less.

- **Changed cell:** amber text on the amber tint was 4.2:1 in light; `amber()` now uses the normal text colour whenever the amber text is under 4.5:1 (also used for the foreground of a changed cell).

Tests: `tests/test_qt_polish_data_specs.py`, `_history_review.py`, `_procmods.py` (light builds, width sharing, toolbar wrap, live switch, contrast pairs).
